. 1. there exists
0<\underline{\lambda}<\overline{\lambda}<\infty
such that
\underline{\lambda}\leq\inf_{(z,\gamma)\in\mathcal{Z}\times\Gamma}\lambda_{min}(\Sigma_{0}(z,\gamma))\leq\sup_{(z,\gamma)\in\mathcal{Z}\times\Gamma}\lambda_{max}(\Sigma_{0}(z,\gamma))\leq\overline{\lambda}
; 2.
\Sigma_{0}(z,\gamma)
is continuously differentiable in
\gamma
in a neighborhood
\Gamma_{0}
of
\gamma_{0}
for each
z\in\mathcal{Z}
with Jacobian satisfying
\sup_{(z,\gamma)\in\mathcal{Z}\times\Gamma_{0}}||\partial vec(\Sigma_{0}(z,\gamma))/\partial\gamma^{\prime}||_{op}<\infty
.

Assumption 8
.

1. the class
\{m_{0}(\cdot,\gamma)^{\prime}\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(\cdot,\tilde{\gamma})m_{0}(\cdot,\gamma):(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma})\in\Gamma\times\Gamma\}
is
P_{0,Z}
-Glivenko-Cantelli; 2. for every
\xi>0
,
\inf_{\gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\gamma}}:||\gamma-\gamma_{0}||_{2}\geq\xi}E_{P_{0,Z}}[||m_{0}(Z,\gamma)||_{2}^{2}]>0
.

Assumption 9
.

Let
V_{0}(Z,\tilde{\gamma})=M_{0}(Z,\gamma_{0})^{\prime}\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(Z,\tilde{\gamma})M_{0}(Z,\gamma_{0})
. 1.
\{V_{0}(\cdot,\tilde{\gamma}):\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma\}
is
P_{0,Z}
-Glivenko-Cantelli; 2.
E_{P_{0,Z}}[M_{0}(Z,\gamma_{0})^{\prime}M_{0}(Z,\gamma_{0})]
is positive definite.

Assumption 10
.

For
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
, there exists a sequence of sets
\{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}_{n\geq 1}
such that

1.

\Pi(p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}|W^{(n)})\longrightarrow 1
in
P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}
-probability as
n\rightarrow\infty

2.

For each
p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
,

(a)

m(z_{i},\gamma)
,
i=1,...,n
, is twice continuously differentiable in
\gamma
over
\Gamma_{0}

(b)

\Sigma(z_{i},\gamma)
,
i=1,...,n
, is once continuously differentiable in
\gamma
over
\Gamma_{0}
,

where
\Gamma_{0}
is the neighborhood of
\gamma_{0}
from Assumption
6
.

3.

The following holds:

(a)

\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||m(\cdot,\gamma)-m_{0}(\cdot,\gamma)||_{n,2}=o(n^{-\frac{1}{4}})

(b)

\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||M(\cdot,\gamma)-M_{0}(\cdot,\gamma)||_{n,2}=o(n^{-\frac{1}{4}})

(c)

\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\Sigma(\cdot,\gamma)-\Sigma_{0}(\cdot,\gamma)||_{n,\infty}=o(n^{-\frac{1}{4}})

(d)

\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\partial\text{vec}(M(\cdot,\gamma)^{\prime})/\partial\gamma^{\prime}||_{n,2}=O(1)

(e)

\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\partial\text{vec}(\Sigma(\cdot,\gamma))/\partial\gamma^{\prime}||_{n,\infty}=O(1)

(f)

\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}E_{P_{W|Z}}[\exp(|t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)|)|Z=z_{i}]=O(1)

for any
t
in a neighborhood of zero.

Assumption 11
.

1.
\mathcal{W}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d_{w}}
is compact; 2.
\mathcal{Z}=[0,1]^{d_{z}}
; 3.
p_{0,W|Z}
is continuous and positive on
\mathcal{W}\times\mathcal{Z}
; 4.
P_{0,Z}
admits a Lebesgue density
p_{0,Z}
that is bounded away from zero and infinity.

Assumption 12
.

1.
(w,z,\gamma)\mapsto g_{j}(w,z,\gamma)
is continuous in all arguments over
\mathcal{W}\times\mathcal{Z}\times\Gamma
for
j=1,...,d_{g}
; 2.
g_{j}(w,z,\gamma)
,
j=1,...,d_{g}
, is differentiable in
\gamma
over a neighborhood
\Gamma_{0}
of
\gamma_{0}
with first and second derivatives continuous in all arguments over
\mathcal{W}\times\mathcal{Z}\times\Gamma_{0}
.

Assumption 13
.

1.
\Theta\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d_{\theta}}
is compact; 2.
(w,z,\theta)\mapsto f_{\theta,j}(w_{j}|w_{1},...,w_{j-1},z)
is continuous in all arguments for all
j=1,...,d_{w}
; 3.
f_{\theta,j}(w_{j}|w_{1},...,w_{j-1},z)>0
for all
(w,z,\theta)\in\mathcal{W}\times\mathcal{Z}\times\Theta
and all
j=1,...,d_{w}
; 4. there is a constant
L>0
such that
\sup_{(w,z)\in\mathcal{W}\times\mathcal{Z}}|\log f_{\theta_{1}}(w|z)-\log f_{\theta_{2}}(w|z)|\leq L||\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}||_{2}
for all
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\Theta
.

Assumption 14
.

\Pi=\Pi_{\Theta}\otimes\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}
, where 1.
\Pi_{\Theta}
is a probability distribution over
\Theta
with a continuous Lebesgue density
\pi_{\Theta}
, and 2.
\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}
is the law
\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}
of a centered Gaussian process
B
in
(C([0,1]^{d},\mathbb{R}),||\cdot||_{\infty})
conditioned on
\{||B||_{\infty}\leq\bar{B}\}
for some large
\bar{B}\in(0,\infty)
with
B\sim\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}
taking values in
(C^{a}([0,1]^{d},\mathbb{R}),||\cdot||_{a})
for every
0<a<\alpha
and
\alpha>d_{z}/2
.

Assumption 15
.

The following holds for almost every realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
and every
n\geq 1
, 1.
\Gamma
is a compact subset of
(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\gamma}},||\cdot||_{2})
, 2.
p_{W|Z}\mapsto Q_{n}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})
is
\mathscr{P}_{W|Z}
-measurable for each
(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma})\in\Gamma\times\Gamma
, 3.
\gamma\mapsto Q_{n}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})
is continuous for every
\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma
and every
p_{W|Z}\in\mathcal{P}_{W|Z}
, and 4.
q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})=\argmin_{\gamma\in\Gamma}Q_{n}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})
is unique and satisfies
q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\text{int}(\Gamma)
for every
\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma
and every
p_{W|Z}\in\mathcal{P}_{W|Z}
.

Assumption 16
.

The following holds for almost every realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
and every
n\geq 1
: 1.
\gamma\mapsto m(z_{i},\gamma)
,
i=1,...,n
, is twice continuously differentiable for each
p_{W|Z}\in\mathcal{P}_{W|Z}
, 2.
\gamma\mapsto\Sigma(z_{i},\gamma)
,
i=1,...,n
, is continuously differentiable for each
p_{W|Z}\in\mathcal{P}_{W|Z}
, 3.
\inf_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\min_{1\leq i\leq n}\lambda_{min}(\Sigma(z_{i},\gamma))>0
for each
p_{W|Z}\in\mathcal{P}_{W|Z}
, and 4.
(x_{1},x_{2})\mapsto Q_{n}(x_{1},x_{2},p_{W|Z})
satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma}\left|\left|\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{1}\partial x_{2}^{\prime}}Q_{n}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\right|\right|_{op}<\inf_{\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma}\lambda_{min}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{1}\partial x_{1}^{\prime}}Q_{n}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\right)

for every
p_{W|Z}\in\mathcal{P}_{W|Z}
.

3.
Proofs of Propositions

3.1.
Proofs of Propositions 1–3

3.1.1.
Proof of Proposition 1

I start with three technical lemmas that specialize Lemma 10, Lemma 1, and Theorem 4 of
Ghosal and van der Vaart (2007)
to my paper. For notation, define Kullback-Leibler neighborhoods

\mathcal{U}_{n}(p_{0,W|Z},\delta)=\{(\theta,B):KL_{n}(\theta,B)<\delta^{2},\ KLV_{n}(\theta,B)<\delta^{2}\},

where

\displaystyle KL_{n}(\theta,B)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{P_{0,W|Z}}\left[\log\left(\frac{p_{0,W|Z}(W|Z)}{p_{\theta,B}(W|Z)}\right)\middle|Z=z_{i}\right]

and

\displaystyle KLV_{n}(\theta,B)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Var_{P_{0,W|Z}}\left[\log\left(\frac{p_{0,W|Z}(W|Z)}{p_{\theta,B}(W|Z)}\right)\middle|Z=z_{i}\right].

are the average Kullback-Leibler divergence and variation, respectively.

Lemma 9
.

Suppose Assumption
4
holds. The following holds for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
: for every
\delta>0
, every probability measure
\bar{\Pi}_{n}
supported on
\mathcal{U}_{n}(p_{0,W|Z},\delta)
, and for every constant
C>0
,

\displaystyle P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}\left(\int\frac{L_{n}(p_{\theta,B})}{L_{n}(p_{0,W|Z})}d\bar{\Pi}_{n}(\theta,B)\leq\exp\left(-(1+C)n\delta^{2}\right)\right)\leq\frac{1}{C^{2}n\delta^{2}}.

Lemma 10
.

Suppose Assumption
4
holds and for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
, there is a sequence
\{\delta_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
with
n\delta_{n}^{2}\rightarrow\infty
and sets
\{\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}_{n\geq 1}
such that, for some constants
\bar{C}>(2+C)
with
C>0
small,
\check{C}>0
, and
\breve{C}>0
, the prior satisfies
\Pi(\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}^{c})\leq\check{C}\exp(-\bar{C}n\delta_{n}^{2})
and
\Pi\left(\mathcal{U}_{n}(p_{0,W|Z},\delta_{n})\right)\geq\breve{C}\exp(-n\delta_{n}^{2})
. Then, for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
,
\Pi(\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}^{c}|W^{(n)})\rightarrow 0
in
P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}
-probability as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Lemma 11
.

Suppose that Assumption
4
holds and for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
, there is a sequence
\{\delta_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
with
\delta_{n}\rightarrow 0
and
n\delta_{n}^{2}\rightarrow\infty
and sets
\{\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}_{n\geq 1}
such that, for some constants
K>0
,
\tilde{C}>0
, and
\bar{C}>(2+C)
with
C>0
small, the following holds

(1)

\displaystyle\Pi\left(\mathcal{U}_{n}(p_{0,W|Z},\delta_{n})\right)\gtrsim\exp(-n\delta_{n}^{2})

(2)

\displaystyle\Pi\left(\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}^{c}\right)\lesssim\exp\left(-\bar{C}n\delta_{n}^{2}\right)

(3)

\displaystyle\log N(\delta_{n}/72,\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z},h_{n})\lesssim\tilde{C}n\delta_{n}^{2}.

Then, for almost-every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
, the posterior satisfies

\displaystyle E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[\Pi(h_{n,2}(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z})\geq D\delta_{n}|W^{(n)})\right]\longrightarrow 0

as
n\rightarrow\infty
for
D>0
large.

Proposition 1
.

Suppose Assumptions
4
,
11
,
13
, and
14
holds. If 1.
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}||b_{0,\theta}||_{\infty}\leq\bar{B}
, 2.
\{b_{0,\theta}:\theta\in\Theta\}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{H}}
, and 3.
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\varphi_{b_{0,\theta}}(\delta_{n})\leq n\delta_{n}^{2}
for some
\{\delta_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
satisfying
\delta_{n}\rightarrow 0
and
n\delta_{n}^{2}\rightarrow\infty
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, then, for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
,
\Pi(h_{n,2}(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z})<D\delta_{n}|W^{(n)})\rightarrow 1
in
P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}
-probability as
n\rightarrow\infty
for some
D>0
.

Proof.

The proof has three steps that amount to verifying Lemma
11
. Step 1 shows (
1
). Step 2 constructs sets
\{\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}_{n\geq 1}
and shows condition (
2
). Step 3 checks (
3
).

Step 1.

The first step is to show (
1
). Applying Lemma 3.1 of
van der Vaart and van Zanten (2008a)
, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\Pi\left(\sup_{(w,z)\in\mathcal{W}\times[0,1]^{d_{z}}}|\log f_{\theta}(w|z)+B(F_{\theta,z}(w),z)-\log p_{0,W|Z}(w|z)|<2\delta_{n}\right)\geq\exp(-n\delta_{n}^{2}).

Applying the law of total probability and that
\Pi=\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}\otimes\Pi_{\Theta}
and Assumption
14
, I know that

\displaystyle\Pi\left(\sup_{(w,z)\in\mathcal{W}\times[0,1]^{d_{z}}}\left|\log f_{\theta}(w|z)+B(F_{\theta,z}(w),z)-\log p_{0,W|Z}(w|z)\right|<2\delta\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\Theta}\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}\left(\sup_{(w,z)\in\mathcal{W}\times[0,1]^{d_{z}}}\left|\log f_{\theta}(w|z)+B(F_{\theta,z}(w),z)-\log p_{0,W|Z}(w|z)\right|<2\delta\right)\pi_{\Theta}(\theta)d\theta

Since
w=F_{\theta,z}^{-1}(F_{\theta,z}(w))
for
\theta
and
z
fixed, I can use the definition of
b_{0,\theta}
and that
F_{\theta,z}(\mathcal{W})=[0,1]^{d_{w}}
to conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{w\in\mathcal{W}}\left|\log f_{\theta}(w|z)+B(F_{\theta,z}(w),z)-\log p_{0,W|Z}(w|z)\right|

\displaystyle=\sup_{w\in\mathcal{W}}\left|B(F_{\theta,z}(w),z)-b_{0,\theta}(F_{\theta,z}(w),z)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{(u,z)\in[0,1]^{d_{w}}\times[0,1]^{d_{z}}}\left|B(u,z)-b_{0,\theta}(u,z)\right|

for all
z\in[0,1]^{d_{z}}
. Taking the supremum over
z\in[0,1]^{d_{z}}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{\Theta}\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}\left(\sup_{(w,z)\in\mathcal{W}\times[0,1]^{d_{z}}}\left|\log f_{\theta}(w|z)+B(F_{\theta,z}(w),z)-\log p_{0,W|Z}(w|z)\right|<2\delta\right)\pi_{\Theta}(\theta)d\theta

\displaystyle\quad\geq\int_{\Theta}\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}\left(\sup_{(u,z)\in[0,1]^{d_{w}}\times[0,1]^{d_{z}}}\left|B(u,z)-b_{0,\theta}(u,z)\right|<2\delta\right)\pi_{\Theta}(\theta)d\theta.

Since
\sup_{(u,z)\in[0,1]^{d_{w}}\times[0,1]^{d_{z}}}|B(u,z)|\leq\bar{B}
implies that

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}\left(||B-b_{0,\theta}||_{\infty}<2\delta\right)=\frac{\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}\left(||B-b_{0,\theta}||_{\infty}<2\delta\right)}{\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(||B||_{\infty}\leq\bar{B})}

for
\delta>0
small, I apply Lemma 5.3 of
van der Vaart and van Zanten (2008b)
to conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{\Theta}\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}\left(||B-b_{0,\theta}||_{\infty}<2\delta\right)\pi_{\Theta}(\theta)d\theta\geq\frac{1}{\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(||B||_{\infty}\leq\bar{B})}\int_{\Theta}\exp(-\varphi_{b_{0,\theta}}(\delta))\pi_{\Theta}(\theta)d\theta

for
\delta>0
small. Consequently, the inequality
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\varphi_{b_{0,\theta}}(\delta_{n})\leq n\delta_{n}^{2}
implies

\displaystyle\Pi\left(\sup_{(w,z)\in\mathcal{W}\times[0,1]^{d_{z}}}|\log f_{\theta}(w|z)+B(F_{\theta,z}(w),z)-\log p_{0,W|Z}(w|z)|<2\delta_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\geq\frac{\exp(-n\delta_{n}^{2})}{\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(||B||_{\infty}\leq\bar{B})}.

Step 2.

I now construct
\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
. Let
\mathcal{B}^{(1)}
be the unit ball in
(C(T,\mathbb{R}),||\cdot||_{\infty})
, let
\mathcal{H}^{(1)}
be the unit ball in
(\mathcal{H},\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\mathcal{H}})
, let
\mathcal{B}_{n}=\delta_{n}\mathcal{B}^{(1)}+D_{n}\mathcal{H}^{(1)}
for
D_{n}=-2\Phi^{-1}(\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(||B||_{\infty}\leq\bar{B})\exp(-Cn\delta_{n}^{2}))
for some
C>1
large, and let
\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}=\{p_{\theta,B}:(\theta,B)\in\Theta\times\mathcal{B}_{n}\}
. Theorem 5.1 of
van der Vaart and van Zanten (2008b)
implies that

\displaystyle\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(B\in\mathcal{B}_{n}^{c})\leq 1-\Phi\left(\Phi^{-1}\left(\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(||B||_{\infty}\leq\delta_{n})\right)+D_{n}\right),

Since
\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(||B||_{\infty}\leq\delta_{n})\geq\exp(-n\delta_{n}^{2})
by Theorem 5.1 of
van der Vaart and van Zanten (2008b)
and
\varphi_{b_{0,\theta}}(\delta_{n})\leq n\delta_{n}^{2}
for all
\theta\in\Theta
, it follows that

\displaystyle\Phi^{-1}\left(\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(||B||_{\infty}\leq\delta_{n})\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Phi^{-1}\left(\exp(-n\delta_{n}^{2})\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Phi^{-1}\left(\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(||B||_{\infty}\leq\bar{B})\exp(-Cn\delta_{n}^{2})\right)

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{D_{n}}{2},

where the second inequality uses that
C>1
and
0<\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(||B||_{\infty}\leq\bar{B})\leq 1
.
Consequently, I conclude that
\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(B\in\mathcal{B}_{n}^{c})\leq 1-\Phi\left(D_{n}/2\right)
and then
\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(B\in\mathcal{B}_{n}^{c})\leq\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}\left(||B||_{\infty}\leq\bar{B}\right)\exp\left(-Cn\delta_{n}^{2}\right)
using the symmetry of the standard normal distribution cumulative distribution function. Since
\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}\left(||B||_{\infty}\leq\bar{B}\right)\leq 1
,
\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}\left(B\in\mathcal{B}_{n}^{c}\right)\leq\exp\left(-Cn\delta_{n}^{2}\right)
. This verifies (
2
).

Step 3.

The final step is to show (
3
). Using the triangle inequality

\displaystyle h(p_{\theta_{1},B_{1}}(\cdot|z_{i}),p_{\theta_{2},B_{2}}(\cdot|z_{i}))

\displaystyle\leq h(p_{\theta_{1},B_{1}}(\cdot|z_{i}),p_{\theta_{1},B_{2}}(\cdot|z_{i}))+h(p_{\theta_{1},B_{2}}(\cdot|z_{i}),p_{\theta_{2},B_{2}}(\cdot|z_{i}))

for
i=1,...,n
. Using Lemma 3.1 of
van der Vaart and van Zanten (2008a)
and Assumption
13
, there is a constant
L>0
such that

\displaystyle h(p_{\theta_{1},B_{2}}(\cdot|z_{i}),p_{\theta_{2},B_{2}}(\cdot|z_{i}))

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{(w,z)\in\mathcal{W}\times[0,1]^{d_{z}}}|\log f_{\theta_{1}}(w|z)-\log f_{\theta_{2}}(w|z)|

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\sup_{(w,z)\in\mathcal{W}\times[0,1]^{d_{z}}}|\log f_{\theta_{1}}(w|z)-\log f_{\theta_{2}}(w|z)|\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq L\cdot||\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}||_{2}\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}L\cdot||\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}||_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq L\cdot||\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}||_{2}\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}L\cdot\text{diam}(\Theta)\right)

for all
i=1,...,n
, where
\text{diam}(\Theta)=\sup_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\Theta}||\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}||_{2}<\infty
(by Assumption
13
.1). Moreover, Lemma 3.1 of
van der Vaart and van Zanten (2008a)
implies that

\displaystyle h(p_{\theta_{1},B_{1}}(\cdot|z),p_{\theta_{1},B_{2}}(\cdot|z))\leq||B_{1}-B_{2}||_{\infty}\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}||B_{1}-B_{2}||_{\infty}\right)

for
i=1,...,n
. Consequently,

\displaystyle h_{n,2}(p_{\theta_{1},B_{1}},p_{\theta_{2},B_{2}})

\displaystyle\leq||B_{1}-B_{2}||_{\infty}\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}||B_{1}-B_{2}||_{\infty}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+L\cdot||\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}||_{2}\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}L\cdot\text{diam}(\Theta)\right),

This bound implies that the
\delta_{n}/72
entropy of
\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
relative to
h_{n}
is bounded by the
\tilde{c}\delta_{n}
entropy of
\Theta\times\mathcal{B}_{n}
with respect to the product norm
||(\theta,B)||=||\theta||_{2}+||B||_{\infty}
, where
\tilde{c}>0
is some constant. Consequently, I bound
\log N(c\delta_{n},\Theta\times\mathcal{B}_{n},||\cdot||)
for any constant
c>0
. For any constant
c>0
,

\displaystyle\log N(c\delta_{n},\Theta\times\mathcal{B}_{n},||\cdot||)

\displaystyle\leq\log N\left(\frac{c\delta_{n}}{2},\Theta,||\cdot||_{2}\right)+\log N\left(\frac{c\delta_{n}}{2},\mathcal{B}_{n},||\cdot||_{\infty}\right)

\displaystyle\leq d_{\theta}\log\left(\frac{2\cdot\text{diam}(\Theta)}{c\delta_{n}}\right)+\log N\left(\frac{c\delta_{n}}{2},\mathcal{B}_{n},||\cdot||_{\infty}\right),

where the first inequality applies a standard inequality relating metric entropy of Cartesian products to the metric entropy of their components, and the second inequality uses the expression for the
||\cdot||_{2}
-metric entropy of a compact subset
\Theta
of
\mathbb{R}^{d_{\theta}}
. Following the arguments of Theorem 2.1 in
van der Vaart and van Zanten (2008a)
, it can also be shown that

\displaystyle\log N\left(\frac{c\delta_{n}}{2},\mathcal{B}_{n},||\cdot||_{\infty}\right)\lesssim n\delta_{n}^{2}

for any
c>0
if
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\varphi_{b_{0,\theta}}(\delta_{n})\leq n\delta_{n}^{2}
. Consequently,
\log N(c\delta_{n},\Theta\times\mathcal{B}_{n},||\cdot||)\lesssim n\delta_{n}^{2}
for any
c>0
, which implies (
3
).
∎

3.1.2.
Proof of Proposition 2

The next lemma and proof of Proposition 2 relies on the theory of wavelets. Let
\{\psi_{jk}:j\geq 0,0\leq k\leq 2^{jd_{z}-1}\}
be the
d_{z}
-dimensional tensor product of the boundary-adapted wavelet basis for
L^{2}([0,1])
from
Cohen et al. (1993)
(‘CDV wavelets’ from hereafter).
1
1

1

There are other ways to express the CDV wavelet basis, however this notation is consistent with the Bayesian nonparametrics literature (e.g.,
Castillo (2014)
,
Castillo and Rousseau (2015)
,
Ray and van der Vaart (2020)
).
The following properties of CDV wavelets are used in the lemma (and the proof of Proposition 2):

•

\{\psi_{j,k}\}
forms an orthonormal basis of
L^{2}([0,1]^{d_{z}})
.

•

||\psi_{jk}||_{\infty}\lesssim 2^{jd_{z}/2}
for all
j,k
.

•

\sum_{k=0}^{2^{jd_{z}-1}}||\psi_{jk}||_{\infty}\lesssim 2^{jd_{z}}
for each
j\geq 1
.

•

\langle\psi_{jk},1\rangle_{L^{2}([0,1]^{d_{z}})}=0
for
j
large.

•

\{\psi_{jk}\}
characterizes Besov spaces
\mathfrak{B}_{\infty,\infty,a}([0,1]^{d_{z}},\mathbb{R})
for
a\leq\alpha
with
\alpha>0
fixed in that
f\in\mathfrak{B}_{\infty,\infty,a}([0,1]^{d_{z}})
iff

||f||_{\infty,\infty,a}:=\sup_{j\geq 0,0\leq k\leq 2^{jd_{z}-1}}2^{j(d_{z}/2+a)}|\langle f,\psi_{jk}\rangle_{L^{2}([0,1]^{d_{z}})}|<\infty.

This last property is useful for Hölder functions because
\mathfrak{B}_{\infty,\infty,a}([0,1]^{d_{z}},\mathbb{R})=C^{a}([0,1]^{d_{z}},\mathbb{R})
for noninteger
a
with equivalent norms, and, for integer
a
,
C^{a}([0,1]^{d_{z}},\mathbb{R})\subseteq\mathfrak{B}_{\infty,\infty,a}([0,1]^{d_{z}},\mathbb{R})
and there exists a universal constant
C>0
such that
||f||_{\infty,\infty,a}\leq C||f||_{a}
(i.e., it is a continuous embedding). I also make use of the following notation:

•

\tilde{\psi}_{jk}(z_{i})=\psi_{jk}(z_{i})-\hat{\tau}_{jk}^{\prime}\psi^{J}_{-jk}(z_{i}),
where
\psi_{-jk}^{J}(z_{i})
is the subvector of
\psi^{J}(z_{i})=(\psi_{jk}(z_{i}):0\leq j\leq J,0\leq k\leq 2^{jd_{z}-1})^{\prime}
that excludes
\psi_{jk}(z_{i})
, and
\hat{\tau}_{jk}
is the sample ordinary least squares coefficients from the linear predictor of
\psi_{jk}
on
\psi_{-jk}^{J}
.

•

\Psi_{J}
is the
n\times d(J)
matrix with rows
\psi^{J}(z_{i})^{\prime}
and
d(J)
is the number of elements of
\psi^{J}(z)
.

Lemma 12
.

Suppose Assumptions
4
and
11
hold, and
2^{J_{n}}=(n/\log n)^{1/(2\alpha+d_{z})}
with
\alpha>d_{z}/2
, then there are
c_{1},c_{2}>0
such that, for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
,
\lambda_{min}\left(n^{-1}\Psi_{J_{n}}^{\prime}\Psi_{J_{n}}\right)\geq c_{1}
and
\min_{1\leq j\leq J_{n}}\min_{0\leq k\leq 2^{jd_{z}}-1}n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\psi}_{jk}^{2}(z_{i})\geq c_{2}
for large
n
.

Proposition 2
.

Suppose Assumptions
4
,
11
,
13
, and
14
hold, the conditions of Proposition
1
hold,
p_{0,W|Z}
satisfies
\int_{\mathcal{W}}||\log p_{0,W|Z}(w|\cdot)||_{\alpha_{0}}dw<\infty
for some
\alpha_{0}>d_{z}/2
, and
\{f_{\theta}:\theta\in\Theta\}
satisfies
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\int_{\mathcal{W}}||\log f_{\theta}(w|\cdot)||_{a}dw<\infty
and
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\int_{\mathcal{W}}||F_{\theta,\cdot}(w)||_{a}^{\lfloor a\rfloor}dw<\infty
for each
a\in(0,\alpha)
. Then, for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
, there exists
\{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{1,n,W|Z}\}_{n\geq 1}
and
\{\tilde{\delta}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
such that
\Pi(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,1,W|Z}|W^{(n)})\rightarrow 1
in
P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}
-probability as
n\rightarrow\infty
and
\sup_{p_{\theta,B}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{1,n,W|Z}}h_{n,\infty}(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z})\leq\tilde{D}\tilde{\delta}_{n}
for a constant
\tilde{D}>0
, where
\tilde{\delta}_{n}
depends on
\alpha,\alpha_{0}
,
d_{z}
, and the RMSH rate
\delta_{n}
.

Proof.

The proof has five steps. Step 1 constructs the sets
\{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{1,n,W|Z}\}_{n\geq 1}
and shows that their posterior probability approaches one as the sample size grows. Step 2 establishes that the empirical supremum
L^{1}
-distance between the sample least squares projections of
p_{\theta,B}
and
p_{0,W|Z}
onto a finite-dimensional linear sieve formed using the wavelet system is bounded from above by the RMSH distance and a multiplicative constant that depends on the sieve dimension. Step 3 and Step 4 derive bounds on the approximation errors. Step 5 concludes.

Step 1.

I show that
\Pi(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{1,n,W|Z}^{c}|W^{(n)})\rightarrow 0
in
P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}
-probability as
n\rightarrow\infty
, where

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{1,n,W|Z}=\{(\theta,B):h_{n,2}(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z})\leq D\delta_{n}\}\cap\{(\theta,B):||B||_{a}\leq D\sqrt{n}\delta_{n},\ ||B||_{\infty}\leq\bar{B}\}

for some
a\in(0,\alpha)
and some
D>0
large. Given Proposition 1 and that
||B||_{\infty}\leq\bar{B}
for
\Pi
-almost every
B
, I need to show that
\Pi((\theta,B):||B||_{a}>D\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}|W^{(n)})\rightarrow 0
in
P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}
-probability as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Since
\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}
is concentrated on functions with elements in
C^{a}([0,1]^{d})
, it can be viewed as the law of a Gaussian process on
C^{a}([0,1]^{d})
, and, as a result, Proposition A.2.1 of
van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
implies that the following holds:
\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(||B||_{a}>D\sqrt{n}\delta_{n})\leq\exp(-Cn\delta_{n}^{2})
for some constant
C>0
, and, as a result,
\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}(||B||_{a}>D\sqrt{n}\delta_{n})\leq\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}^{-1}(||B||_{\infty}\leq\bar{B})\exp(-Cn\delta_{n}^{2})
. Applying Lemma
10
(with
\Theta\times\{B:||B||_{a}>D\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}\}
in place of
\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
) yields the posterior concentration
\Pi((\theta,B):||B||_{a}>D\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}|W^{(n)})\rightarrow 0
in
P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}
-probability as
n\rightarrow\infty
for
D>0
large.

Step 2.

Let
J=(n/\log n)^{1/(2\alpha+d_{z})}
and suppose that
n
is sufficiently large so that the bounds in Lemma
12
hold. Let
\hat{p}^{J}_{\theta,B}(w,z)=\sum_{j=0}^{J}\sum_{k=0}^{2^{jd_{z}}-1}\psi_{jk}(z)\hat{\beta}_{jk}^{J}(w)
and
\hat{p}^{J}_{0,W|Z}(w,z)=\sum_{j=0}^{J}\sum_{k=0}^{2^{jd_{z}}-1}\psi_{jk}(z)\hat{\beta}_{0,jk}^{J}(w)
be the
sample
least squares projections of
p_{\theta,B}(w|\cdot)
and
p_{0,W|Z}(w|\cdot)
onto the linear space generated by
\{\psi_{jk}:0\leq j\leq J,0\leq k\leq 2^{jd_{z}}-1\}
, respectively. Applying the triangle inequality and
\sum_{k=0}^{2^{jd_{z}}-1}||\psi_{jk}||_{\infty}\lesssim 2^{jd_{z}/2}
, I know that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{W}}|\hat{p}^{J}_{\theta,B}(w,z_{i})-\hat{p}_{0}^{J}(w,z_{i})|dw

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{J}\max_{0\leq k\leq 2^{jd_{z}}-1}\int_{\mathcal{W}}\left|\hat{\beta}_{jk}^{J}(w)-\hat{\beta}_{0,jk}^{J}(w)\right|dw\sum_{k=0}^{2^{jd_{z}}-1}||\psi_{jk}||_{\infty}

(4)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{J}2^{\frac{jd_{z}}{2}}\max_{0\leq k\leq 2^{jd_{z}}-1}\int_{\mathcal{W}}\left|\hat{\beta}_{jk}^{J}(w)-\hat{\beta}_{0,jk}^{J}(w)\right|dw

for any
J\geq 1
. Consequently, I seek a uniform bound (in
j,k
) for
\int_{\mathcal{W}}|\hat{\beta}_{jk}^{J}(w)-\hat{\beta}_{0,jk}^{J}(w)|dw
. Using residual regression (cf. Chapter 17.3 of
Goldberger (1991)
), I know that

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{jk}^{J}(w)-\hat{\beta}_{0,jk}^{J}(w)=\frac{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\psi}_{jk}(z_{i})\left(p_{\theta,B}(w|z_{i})-p_{0,W|Z}(w|z_{i})\right)}{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\psi}_{jk}^{2}(z_{i})}.

The triangle inequality, Hölder’s inequality, and the definition of the minimum then implies that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{W}}|\hat{\beta}_{jk}^{J}(w)-\hat{\beta}_{0,jk}^{J}(w)|dw

\displaystyle\quad=\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\psi}_{jk}^{2}(z_{i})\right)^{-1}\int_{\mathcal{W}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\psi}_{jk}(z_{i})(p_{\theta,B}(w|z_{i})-p_{0,W|Z}(w|z_{i}))\right|dw

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\psi}_{jk}^{2}(z_{i})\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\tilde{\psi}_{jk}(z_{i})\right|\cdot\int_{\mathcal{W}}\left|p_{\theta,B}(w|z_{i})-p_{0,W|Z}(w|z_{i})\right|dw

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\psi}_{jk}^{2}(z_{i})\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\int_{\mathcal{W}}|p_{\theta,B}(w|z_{i})-p_{0,W|Z}(w|z_{i})|dw\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\left(\min_{0\leq j\leq J}\min_{0\leq k\leq 2^{jd_{z}-1}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\psi}_{jk}^{2}(z_{i})\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}h_{n,2}(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z}),

where the last inequality uses the equivalence between the total variation distance and the Hellinger metric. Substituting this into the upper bound (
4
) and applying Lemma
12
, there is a constant
\tilde{c}>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\theta,B)\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{1,n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\int_{\mathcal{W}}|\hat{p}^{J}_{\theta,B}(w,z_{i})-\hat{p}_{0,W|Z}^{J}(w,z_{i})|dw\leq\tilde{c}2^{\frac{Jd_{z}}{2}}\delta_{n}.

Step 3.

Applying the triangle inequality, I know that

\displaystyle|p_{0,W|Z}(w|z_{i})-\hat{p}^{J}_{0,W|Z}(w,z_{i})|

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\left|p_{0,W|Z}(w|z_{i})-p^{J}_{0,W|Z}(w,z_{i})\right|}_{T_{1}(w,z_{i})}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\left|p^{J}_{0,W|Z}(w,z_{i})-\hat{p}^{J}_{0,W|Z}(w,z_{i})\right|}_{T_{2}(w,z_{i})},

where
p_{0,W|Z}^{J}(w,z)=\sum_{j=0}^{J}\sum_{k=0}^{2^{jd_{z}}-1}\psi_{jk}(z)\beta_{0,jk}(w)
is
population
least squares projection of
p_{0,W|Z}(w|\cdot)
onto
\text{span}\{\psi_{lk}:0\leq j\leq J,0\leq k\leq 2^{jd_{z}}-1\}
. Since 1.
P_{0,Z}
has a Lebesgue density on
[0,1]^{d_{z}}
that is bounded away from zero and infinity, and 2.
p_{0,W|Z}
is bounded away from zero and infinity, there is a constant
\tilde{C}>0
independent of
w
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in[0,1]^{d_{z}}}T_{1}(w,z)\leq\tilde{C}2^{-\alpha_{0}J}||\log p_{0,W|Z}(w|\cdot)||_{\alpha_{0}},

by standard uniform approximation theory for tensor products of CDV wavelets (e.g.,
Chen (2007)
) and the chain rule. Consequently, the condition

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{W}}||\log p_{0,W|Z}(w|\cdot)||_{\alpha_{0}}dw<\infty

implies

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\int_{\mathcal{W}}T_{1}(w,z_{i})dw\leq\tilde{\tilde{C}}2^{-\alpha_{0}J}

for some constant
\tilde{\tilde{C}}>0
. For
T_{2}(w,z_{i})
, I first apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to obtain that

\displaystyle T_{2}(w,z_{i})\leq\sqrt{\sum_{j=0}^{J}\sum_{k=0}^{2^{jd_{z}}-1}\psi_{jk}^{2}(z_{i})}\sqrt{\sum_{j=0}^{J}\sum_{k=0}^{2^{jd_{z}-1}}|\hat{\beta}_{0,jk}(w)-\beta_{0,jk}(w)|^{2}}

Since
||\psi_{jk}||_{\infty}\lesssim 2^{jd_{z}/2}
for all
j,k
and
\sum_{k=0}^{2^{jd_{z}}-1}||\psi_{jk}||_{\infty}\lesssim 2^{jd_{z}/2}
, I can conclude that
T_{2}(w,z_{i})\lesssim 2^{\frac{Jd_{z}}{2}}||\hat{\beta}_{0}^{J}(w)-\beta_{0}^{J}(w)||_{2}
, where
\hat{\beta}_{0}^{J}(w)
is the vector of projection coefficients
(\hat{\beta}_{0,jk}^{J}(w):0\leq j\leq J,\ 0\leq k\leq 2^{jd_{z}-1})^{\prime}
and
\beta^{J}_{0}(w)
is defined similarly. Let
\varepsilon(w)
be the
n\times 1
vector with
i
th element
\varepsilon_{i}(w)=p_{0,W|Z}(w|z_{i})-p^{J}_{0,W|Z}(w,z_{i})
,
i=1,...,n
. Since

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{0}^{J}(w)=\left(\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J}^{\prime}\Psi_{J}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J}^{\prime}Y(w)\right)

with
Y(w)
being the
n\times 1
vector with element
p_{0,W|Z}(w|z_{i})
for
i=1,...,n
, standard least squares algebra reveals that

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{0}^{J}(w)=\beta_{0}^{J}(w)+\left(\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J}^{\prime}\Psi_{J}\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J}^{\prime}\varepsilon(w),

Let
\hat{\varepsilon}_{J}(w)=\Psi_{J}(\Psi_{J}^{\prime}\Psi_{J})^{-1}\Psi_{J}^{\prime}\varepsilon(w)
. Using the variational characterization of eigenvalues, the fact that
\varepsilon(w)=\hat{\varepsilon}_{J}(w)+(\varepsilon(w)-\hat{\varepsilon}_{J}(w))
with
\varepsilon(w)-\hat{\varepsilon}_{J}(w)
orthogonal to
\hat{\varepsilon}_{J}(w)
in the empirical
L^{2}
inner product, and the fact that projection is norm reducing, I obtain

\displaystyle||\hat{\beta}_{0}^{J}(w)-\beta_{0}^{J}(w)||_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=\left(\frac{1}{n}\varepsilon(w)^{\prime}\Psi_{J}\right)\left(\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J}^{\prime}\Psi_{J}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J}^{\prime}\Psi_{J}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J}^{\prime}\Psi_{J}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J}^{\prime}\varepsilon(w)\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left(\lambda_{min}\left(\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J}^{\prime}\Psi_{J}\right)\right)^{-1}\left|\left|\left(\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J}^{\prime}\Psi_{J}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J}^{\prime}\varepsilon(w)\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=\left(\lambda_{min}\left(\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J}^{\prime}\Psi_{J}\right)\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\varepsilon(w)^{\prime}\hat{\varepsilon}_{J}(w)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left(\lambda_{min}\left(\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J}^{\prime}\Psi_{J}\right)\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\hat{\varepsilon}_{J}(w)^{\prime}\hat{\varepsilon}_{J}(w)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left(\lambda_{min}\left(\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J}^{\prime}\Psi_{J}\right)\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\varepsilon(w)^{\prime}\varepsilon(w).

Consequently, I obtain

\displaystyle T_{2}(w,z_{i})

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\lambda_{min}\left(\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J}^{\prime}\Psi_{J}\right)\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}2^{\frac{Jd_{z}}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\varepsilon(w)^{\prime}\varepsilon(w)}.

for all
i=1,...,n
. Since
n^{-1}\varepsilon(w)^{\prime}\varepsilon(w)=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|T_{1}(w,z_{i})|^{2}
, I know that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\varepsilon(w)^{\prime}\varepsilon(w)}\leq\tilde{C}2^{-\alpha_{0}J}||\log p_{0,W|Z}(w|\cdot)||_{\alpha_{0}}.

Consequently, I obtain that there is a constant
C^{\dagger}>0
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{W}}T_{2}(w,z_{i})dw\leq C^{\dagger}2^{J(\frac{d_{z}}{2}-\alpha_{0})}\left(\lambda_{min}\left(\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J}^{\prime}\Psi_{J}\right)\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}

for all
i=1,...,n
. Putting everything together and applying Lemma
12
, I obtain that

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\int_{\mathcal{W}}|p_{0,W|Z}(w|z_{i})-\hat{p}_{0,W|Z}^{J}(w,z_{i})|dw\leq\acute{C}(2^{J(\frac{d_{z}}{2}-\alpha_{0})}+2^{-\alpha_{0}J}).

for some constant
\acute{C}>0
.

Step 4.

Like Step 3, I apply the triangle inequality to conclude that

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\int_{\mathcal{W}}|p_{\theta,B}(w|z_{i})-\hat{p}^{J}_{\theta,B}(w,z_{i})|dw

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\int_{\mathcal{W}}|p_{\theta,B}(w|z_{i})-p^{J}_{\theta,B}(w,z_{i})|dw

\displaystyle\quad+\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\int_{\mathcal{W}}|p^{J}_{\theta,B}(w,z_{i})-\hat{p}^{J}_{\theta,B}(w,z_{i})|dw,

where
p^{J}_{\theta,B}
is the population least squares projection of
p_{\theta,B}
onto the
\text{span}\{\psi_{lk}:0\leq j\leq J,0\leq k\leq 2^{jd_{z}}-1\}
. Following a symmetric argument to the previous step, I can show that
|p_{\theta,B}(w|z)-p_{\theta,B}^{J}(w,z)|\leq\breve{C}2^{-aJ}||\log p_{\theta,B}(w|\cdot)||_{a}
for all
z\in\mathcal{Z}
, where
\breve{C}>0
is a constant independent of
w
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\int_{\mathcal{W}}|p_{\theta,B}(w|z)-p^{J}_{\theta,B}(w,z)|dw\leq\breve{C}2^{-aJ}\int_{\mathcal{W}}||\log p_{\theta,B}(w|\cdot)||_{a}dw

Since
\log p_{\theta,B}(w|\cdot)=\log f_{\theta}(w|\cdot)+B(F_{\theta,\cdot}(w),\cdot)-\log I(\cdot,B)
with

I(z,B)=\int_{[0,1]^{d_{w}}}\exp(B(u,z))du,

the triangle inequality reveals

\displaystyle||\log p_{\theta,B}(w|\cdot)||_{a}\leq||\log f_{\theta}(w|\cdot)||_{a}+||\log I(\cdot,B)||_{a}+||B(F_{\theta,\cdot}(w),\cdot)||_{a}.

By assumption
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\int_{\mathcal{W}}||\log f_{\theta}(w|\cdot)||_{a}dw<\infty
, and, as a result, the independence of this quantity from
B
implies

\displaystyle\sup_{(\theta,B)\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{1,n,W|Z}}\int_{\mathcal{W}}||\log f_{\theta}(w|\cdot)||_{a}dw<\infty.

Turning to the second term, since
||B||_{\infty}\leq\bar{B}
implies
I(\cdot,B)
is bounded away from zero and infinity, compositions of Hölder functions and smooth functions (i.e.
x\mapsto\log x
) reveals

\displaystyle||\log I(\cdot,B)||_{a}\leq\bar{C}_{1}(||I(\cdot,B)||_{a}+1)

with
\bar{C}_{1}>0
being a constant depending on
a
,
\bar{B}
,
d_{w}
, and
d_{z}
. Similarly, using that
||B||_{\infty}\leq\bar{B}
implies
\exp(B)
is bounded away from zero and infinity, interchanging integration and differentiation, and using compositions of Hölder functions and smooth functions (i.e.
x\mapsto\exp(x)
), I obtain

\displaystyle||I(\cdot,B)||_{a}\leq\bar{C}_{2}(||B||_{a}+1)

with
\bar{C}_{2}>0
also being a constant depending on
a
,
\bar{B}
,
d_{w}
, and
d_{z}
(note the Hölder norm on the RHS is actually that of
C^{a}([0,1]^{d},\mathbb{R})
rather than
C^{a}([0,1]^{d_{z}},\mathbb{R})
). Consequently,

\displaystyle||\log I(\cdot,B)||_{a}\leq\bar{C}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}(||B||_{a}+1)+\bar{C}_{1}

Consequently, I can use that
||B||_{a}\leq D\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}
to conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\theta,B)\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{1,n,W|Z}}\int_{\mathcal{W}}||\log I(\cdot,B)||_{a}dw\leq\bar{C}(\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}+1)

where
\bar{C}>0
is a constant that depends on
\bar{B},\alpha
,
d_{z}
,
d_{w}
, the Lebesgue measure of
\mathcal{W}
, and
D
. For the third term, I note that, for
w
and
\theta
fixed,
B(F_{\theta,\cdot}(w),\cdot)
can be written as a composition
B\circ G_{\theta,w}:[0,1]^{d_{z}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, where
G_{\theta,w}(z)=(F_{\theta,z}(w),z)
. Consequently, repeated applications of the chain rule reveal that

\displaystyle||B(F_{\theta,\cdot}(w),\cdot)||_{a}

\displaystyle\leq\grave{C}||B||_{a}(1+||F_{\theta,\cdot}(w)||^{\lfloor a\rfloor}_{a})

where
\grave{C}>0
is a constant that depends on
a
,
d_{w}
, and
d_{z}
. This implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\theta,B)\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{1,n,W|Z}}\int_{\mathcal{W}}||B(F_{\theta,\cdot}(w),\cdot)||_{a}dw\leq\grave{C}\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}+\grave{C}\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\int_{\mathcal{W}}||F_{\theta,\cdot}(w)||^{\lfloor a\rfloor}_{a}dw,

with the integral in the second term being finite by the assumptions of the proposition. Putting all this together,

\displaystyle\sup_{(\theta,B)\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{1,n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\int_{\mathcal{W}}|p_{\theta,B}(w|z_{i})-p_{\theta,B}^{J}(w,z_{i})|dw\leq\ddot{C}_{1}(\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}2^{-aJ}+2^{-aJ})

for some constant
\ddot{C}_{1}>0
. Given this result, a symmetric argument to Step 3 can be used to conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\theta,B)\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{1,n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\int_{\mathcal{W}}|p^{J}_{\theta,B}(w,z_{i})-\hat{p}^{J}_{\theta,B}(w,z_{i})|dw\leq\ddot{C}_{2}(\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}2^{J(\frac{d_{z}}{2}-a)}+2^{J(\frac{d_{z}}{2}-a)}).

for some constant
\ddot{C}_{2}>0
.

Step 5.

Combining all of these steps via the triangle inequality, I can conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\theta,B)\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{1,n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\int_{\mathcal{W}}\left|p_{\theta,B}(w|z_{i})-p_{0,W|Z}(w|z_{i})\right|dw

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim 2^{\frac{Jd_{z}}{2}}\delta_{n}+2^{J(\frac{d_{z}}{2}-\alpha_{0})}+\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}2^{J(\frac{d_{z}}{2}-a)}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+2^{J(\frac{d_{z}}{2}-a)}+2^{-\alpha_{0}J}+\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}2^{-\alpha J}+2^{-aJ}

Define

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}_{n}=2^{\frac{Jd_{z}}{2}}\delta_{n}+2^{J(\frac{d_{z}}{2}-\alpha_{0})}+\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}2^{J(\frac{d_{z}}{2}-a)}+2^{J(\frac{d_{z}}{2}-a)}+2^{-\alpha_{0}J}+\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}2^{-\alpha J}+2^{-aJ}

and use the equivalence between Hellinger distance and
L^{1}
distance to conclude the proof.
∎

3.1.3.
Proof of Proposition 3

Proposition 3
.

Suppose Assumptions
4
,
5
,
11
,
12
,
13
,
14
, and the conditions of Proposition
1
hold. Further, suppose there are sequences
\{\tilde{g}_{n,\theta,j}^{*}:\theta\in\Theta\}_{n\geq 1}
,
j=1,...,d_{\gamma},
in
\mathcal{H}
and
\{\zeta_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
in
\mathbb{R}_{++}
with
\zeta_{n}\rightarrow 0
such that 1.
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\max_{1\leq j\leq d_{\gamma}}||\tilde{g}_{n,\theta,j}^{*}-\tilde{g}_{0,\theta,j}||_{\infty}\lesssim\zeta_{n}
, 2.
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\max_{1\leq j\leq d_{\gamma}}||\tilde{g}_{n,\theta,j}^{*}||_{\mathcal{H}}\lesssim\sqrt{n}\zeta_{n}
, 3.
\sqrt{n}\zeta_{n}\delta_{n}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, where
\delta_{n}
is the RMSH rate, and 4.
\{\Delta_{n,\theta}^{*}:\theta\in\Theta\}
with
\Delta_{n,\theta}^{*}(w,z)=\tilde{g}_{n,\theta}^{*}(F_{\theta,z}(w),z)-\tilde{g}_{0,\theta}(F_{\theta,z}(w),z)
for
(w,z)\in\mathcal{W}\times[0,1]^{d_{z}}
satisfies, for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\{\Delta_{n,\theta}^{*}(W_{i},z_{i})-E_{P_{0,W|Z}}[\Delta_{n,\theta}^{*}(W,Z)|Z=z_{i}]\}\right|\right|_{2}\overset{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}{\longrightarrow}0

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Then, for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
, there is
\{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}_{n\geq 1}
such that
\Pi(p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}|W^{(n)})\rightarrow 1
in
P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}
-probability and (4) from the Main Text holds.
2
2

2

Recall that (4) from the Main Text is, for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\int_{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}L_{n}(p_{t,n,W|Z})d\Pi(p_{W|Z})}{\int_{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}L_{n}(p_{W|Z})d\Pi(p_{W|Z})}=1+o_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}(1)

for
t
in a neighborhood of zero,
p_{t,n,W|Z}\propto p_{W|Z}\exp\left(-t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0}/\sqrt{n}\right)
, and, for
this
proposition,
p_{W|Z}=p_{\theta,B}
.

Proof.

The goal is to show that, for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
, there are sets
\{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}_{n\geq 1}
for which
\Pi(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}|W^{(n)})1+o_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}(1)
and

\displaystyle\frac{\int_{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}L_{n}(p_{t,n,\theta,B})d\Pi(p_{W|Z})}{\int_{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}L_{n}(p_{W|Z})d\Pi(p_{W|Z})}=1+o_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}(1),

where
p_{t,n,\theta,B}(w|z)\propto p_{\theta,B}(w|z)\exp(-t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}(F_{\theta,z}(w),z)/\sqrt{n})
for each
(w,z)\in\mathcal{W}\times[0,1]^{d_{z}}
and
\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}=-V_{0}^{-1}\tilde{g}_{0,\theta}
.
3
3

3

Since
\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}(F_{\theta,z}(w),z)=\tilde{\chi}_{0}
, the conditional density
p_{t,n,\theta,B}
is exactly that of (4) in the Main Text.

To that end, Step 1 defines sets
\{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}_{n\geq 1}
and shows that the posterior concentrates on them. Step 2 shows that
L_{n}(p_{t,n,\theta,B})
can be replaced with
L_{n}(p_{t,n,\theta,B}^{*})
, where
p_{t,n,\theta,B}^{*}(w|z)\propto p_{\theta,B}(w|z)\exp(-t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}(F_{\theta,z}(w),z)/\sqrt{n})
for each
(w,z)\in\mathcal{W}\times[0,1]^{d_{z}}
, with
\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}=-V_{0}^{-1}\tilde{g}_{n,\theta}^{*}
. Step 3 applies the Cameron-Martin theorem to show that the ratio of integrated likelihoods is asymptotically equivalent to a ratio of posterior probabilities. Step 4 shows that this ratio converges in probability to one.

Step 1.

Since the elements of
\tilde{g}_{n,\theta}^{*}
are contained in
\mathcal{H}
, the elements of
\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}
are also contained in
\mathcal{H}
because they are finite linear combinations of
\tilde{g}_{n,\theta}^{*}
. Let
H_{n,\theta}^{*}
be a
d_{\gamma}\times d_{\gamma}
positive definite matrix with elements
H_{jk,n,\theta}^{*}=\langle\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta,j}^{*},\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta,k}^{*}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}
. Let
G_{n,t,\theta}^{*}(B)
be a random variable that satisfies
G_{n,t,\theta}^{*}(B)\sim\mathcal{N}(0,t^{\prime}H_{n,\theta}^{*}t)
under
\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}
for any
t
in a neighborhood of zero. I define a sequence of sets
\{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,2,W|Z}\}_{n\geq 1}
that satisfy

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,2,W|Z}=\left\{(\theta,B):|G_{n,t,\theta}^{*}(B)|\leq D\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}\sqrt{t^{\prime}H_{n,\theta}^{*}t}\right\}

for each
n\geq 1
. Since
\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}(A)=\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(A\cap\{||B||_{\infty}\leq\bar{B}\})/\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(||B||_{\infty}\leq\bar{B})
, it follows from the law of total probability that

\displaystyle\Pi\left((\theta,B):\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left|G_{n,t,\theta}^{*}(B)\right|\geq D\delta_{n}\sqrt{t^{\prime}H_{n,\theta}^{*}t}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\Theta}\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}\left(B:\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left|G_{n,t,\theta}^{*}(B)\right|\geq D\delta_{n}\sqrt{t^{\prime}H_{n,\theta}^{*}t}\right)\pi_{\Theta}(\theta)d\theta

\displaystyle\quad\quad\leq\frac{\int_{\Theta}\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}\left(B:\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left|G_{n,t,\theta}^{*}(B)\right|\geq D\delta_{n}\sqrt{t^{\prime}H_{n,\theta}^{*}t}\right)\pi_{\Theta}(\theta)d\theta}{\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(B:||B||_{\infty}\leq\bar{B})}

Since
G_{n,t,\theta}^{*}(B)\sim\mathcal{N}(0,t^{\prime}H_{n,\theta}^{*}t)
under
\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}
for
\theta
fixed, it follows that

\displaystyle\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}\left(B:\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left|G_{n,t,\theta}^{*}(B)\right|\geq D\delta_{n}\sqrt{t^{\prime}H_{n,\theta}^{*}t}\right)=1-\Phi(D\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}).

for all
\theta\in\Theta
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\Pi\left((\theta,B):\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left|G_{n,t,\theta}^{*}(B)\right|\geq D\delta_{n}\sqrt{t^{\prime}H_{n,\theta}^{*}t}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{D^{2}n\delta_{n}^{2}}{2}\right)

by the Gaussian tail bound. Lemma
10
then implies
\Pi(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,2,W|Z}^{c}|W^{(n)})\rightarrow 0
in
P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}
-probability as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Combining this with Step 1 of Proposition 2, it follows that
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}=\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,1,W|Z}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,2,W|Z},
where
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,1,W|Z}
is defined in Proposition 2, satisfies

\Pi(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}^{c}|W^{(n)})\overset{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}{\longrightarrow}0

as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Step 2.

I show that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\theta,B)\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}|\log L_{n}(p_{t,n,\theta,B})-\log L_{n}(p_{t,n,\theta,B}^{*})|\overset{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}{\longrightarrow}0

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Direct calculations reveal that

\displaystyle\log L_{n}(p_{t,n,\theta,B})-\log L_{n}(p_{t,n,\theta,B}^{*})

\displaystyle=t^{\prime}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}(F_{\theta,z_{i}}(W_{i}),z_{i})-\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}(F_{\theta,z_{i}}(W_{i}),z_{i})]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log E_{P_{\theta,B}}[\exp(-t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}(F_{\theta,Z}(W),Z)/\sqrt{n})|Z=z_{i}]

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log E_{P_{\theta,B}}[\exp(-t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}(F_{\theta,Z}(W),Z)/\sqrt{n})|Z=z_{i}].

Since
E_{P_{0,W|Z}}[\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}(F_{\theta,Z}(W),Z)|Z=z_{i}]=E_{P_{0,W|Z}}[\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)|Z=z_{i}]=0
for
i=1,...,n
by the definition of
\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}
and Assumption
5
, it follows that

\displaystyle t^{\prime}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}(F_{\theta,z_{i}}(W_{i}),z_{i})-\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}(F_{\theta,z_{i}}(W_{i}),z_{i})]

\displaystyle\quad=t^{\prime}V_{0}^{-1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{n,\theta}(W_{i},z_{i})+t^{\prime}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{P_{0,W|Z}}[\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}(F_{\theta,Z}(W),Z)|Z=z_{i}]

\displaystyle\quad=t^{\prime}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{P_{0,W|Z}}[\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}(F_{\theta,Z}(W),Z)|Z=z_{i}]+o_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}(1)

uniformly over
\Theta
by assumption. Moreover, a Taylor expansion around
t=0
, the fact that
\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}=V_{0}^{-1}\tilde{g}_{n,\theta}^{*}
and
\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}=V_{0}^{-1}\tilde{g}_{0,\theta}
and the assumption that
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}||\tilde{g}_{n,\theta}^{*}-\tilde{g}_{0,\theta}||_{\infty}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log E_{P_{\theta,B}}[\exp(-t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}(F_{\theta,Z}(W),Z)/\sqrt{n}|Z=z_{i}]

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log E_{P_{\theta,B}}[\exp(-t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}(F_{\theta,Z}(W),Z)/\sqrt{n}|Z=z_{i}]

\displaystyle\quad=-t^{\prime}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{P_{\theta,B}}[\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}(F_{\theta,Z}(W),Z)|Z=z_{i}]

\displaystyle\quad\quad+t^{\prime}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{P_{\theta,B}}[\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}(F_{\theta,Z}(W),Z)|Z=z_{i}]+o(1)

uniformly over
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\sup_{(\theta,B)\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\log L_{n}(p_{t,n,\theta,B})-\log L_{n}(p_{t,n,\theta,B}^{*})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{(\theta,B)\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int(\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}(F_{\theta,z_{i}}(w),z_{i})-\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}(F_{\theta,z_{i}}(w),z_{i}))(p_{\theta,B}(w|z_{i})-p_{0,W|Z}(w|z_{i}))dw\right|

\displaystyle\quad+o_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}(1)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{n}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}||\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}-\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}||_{\infty}\sup_{(\theta,B)\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||p_{\theta,B}(\cdot|z_{i})-p_{0,W|Z}(\cdot|z_{i})||_{L^{1}}+o_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}(1)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{n}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}||\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}-\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}||_{\infty}\sup_{(\theta,B)\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||p_{\theta,B}(\cdot|z_{i})-p_{0,W|Z}(\cdot|z_{i})||_{L^{1}}^{2}}+o_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}(1)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{n}\zeta_{n}\delta_{n}+o_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}(1),

where the first inequality is the triangle inequality the results derived earlier, the third is Hölder’s inequality, and the final inequality uses the equivalence between total variation and Hellinger distances as well as the assumption that
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}||\tilde{g}_{n,\theta}^{*}-\tilde{g}_{0,\theta}||_{\infty}\leq\zeta_{n}
(and that
\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}=-V_{0}^{-1}\tilde{g}_{0,\theta}
and
\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}=-V_{0}^{-1}g_{n,\theta}^{*}
). Consequently, the assumption that
\sqrt{n}\zeta_{n}\delta_{n}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
implies

\displaystyle\sup_{(\theta,B)\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\log L_{n}(p_{t,n,\theta,B})-\log L_{n}(p_{t,n,\theta,B}^{*})\right|\overset{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}{\longrightarrow}0

as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Step 3.

Using the result from Step 1 and 2, it follows that

(5)

\displaystyle\frac{\int_{\mathcal{P}_{n,W|Z}}L_{n}(p_{t,n,\theta,B})d\Pi(\theta,B)}{\int_{\mathcal{P}_{n,W|Z}}L_{n}(p_{\theta,B})d\Pi(\theta,B)}=(1+o_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}(1))\frac{\int_{\mathcal{P}_{n,W|Z}}L_{n}(p_{t,n,\theta,B}^{*})d\Pi(\theta,B)}{\int_{\mathcal{P}_{n,W|Z}}L_{n}(p_{\theta,B})d\Pi(\theta,B)}

Letting
c(u,z,b)=\exp(b(u,z))/\int_{[0,1]^{d_{w}}}\exp(b(\tilde{u},z))d\tilde{u}
, the ratio on the RHS of (
5
) satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{\int_{\mathcal{P}_{n,W|Z}}L_{n}(p_{t,n,\theta,B}^{*})d\Pi(\theta,B)}{\int_{\mathcal{P}_{n,W|Z}}L_{n}(p_{\theta,B})d\Pi(\theta,B)}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{\int_{\mathcal{P}_{n,W|Z}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}f_{\theta}(W_{i}|z_{i})C(F_{\theta,z_{i}}(W_{i}),z_{i},B-t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n})d\Pi(\theta,B)}{\int_{\mathcal{P}_{n,W|Z}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}f_{\theta}(W_{i}|z_{i})C(F_{\theta,z_{i}}(W_{i}),z_{i},B)d\Pi(\theta,B)},

Under Assumption
14
, I then conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{\int_{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}f_{\theta}(W_{i}|z_{i})C(F_{\theta,z_{i}}(W_{i}),z_{i},B-t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n})d\Pi(\theta,B)}{\int_{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}f_{\theta}(W_{i}|z_{i})C(F_{\theta,z_{i}}(W_{i}),z_{i},B)d\Pi(\theta,B)}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{\int_{\Theta}\prod_{i=1}^{n}f_{\theta}(W_{i}|z_{i})\int\delta_{\theta,B}(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z})\prod_{i=1}^{n}C(F_{\theta,z_{i}}(W_{i}),z_{i},B-t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n})d\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(B)\pi_{\Theta}(\theta)d\theta}{\int_{\Theta}\prod_{i=1}^{n}f_{\theta}(W_{i}|z_{i})\int\delta_{\theta,B}(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z})\prod_{i=1}^{n}C(F_{\theta,z_{i}}(W_{i}),z_{i},B)d\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(B)\pi_{\Theta}(\theta)d\theta},

where
\delta_{\theta,B}(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z})=\mathbf{1}\{(\theta,B)\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}
. Note that the integrals are taken with respect to
\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}
because
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
imposes
||B||_{\infty}\leq\bar{B}
and
1/\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(||B||_{\infty}\leq\bar{B})
is a common positive constant in the numerator and the denominator. Applying the change of variables
\upsilon=B-t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\int_{\Theta}\prod_{i=1}^{n}f_{\theta}(W_{i}|z_{i})\int\delta_{\theta,B}(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z})\prod_{i=1}^{n}C(F_{\theta,z_{i}}(W_{i}),z_{i},B-t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n})d\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(B)\pi_{\Theta}(\theta)d\theta}{\int_{\Theta}\prod_{i=1}^{n}f_{\theta}(W_{i}|z_{i})\int\delta_{\theta,B}(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z})\prod_{i=1}^{n}C(F_{\theta,z_{i}}(W_{i}),z_{i},B)d\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(B)\pi_{\Theta}(\theta)d\theta}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{\int_{\Theta}\prod_{i=1}^{n}f_{\theta}(W_{i}|z_{i})\int\delta_{\theta,v+t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n}}(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z})\prod_{i=1}^{n}C(F_{\theta,z_{i}}(W_{i}),z_{i},\upsilon)d\tilde{\Pi}_{t,n,\theta,\mathcal{B}}(\upsilon)\pi_{\Theta}(\theta)d\theta}{\int_{\Theta}\prod_{i=1}^{n}f_{\theta}(W_{i}|z_{i})\int\delta_{\theta,B}(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z})\prod_{i=1}^{n}C(F_{\theta,z_{i}}(W_{i}),z_{i},B)d\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(B)\pi_{\Theta}(\theta)d\theta},

where
\tilde{\Pi}_{t,n,\theta,\mathcal{B}}
is the probability law of
B-t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n}
under
\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}
for
\theta
fixed. Since
\{\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta,j}^{*}:\theta\in\Theta,j=1,...,d_{\gamma}\}\subseteq\mathcal{H}
for all
n\geq 1
, I know that
t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}\in\mathcal{H}
for all
\theta\in\Theta
(as a linear space), and, as a result, the Cameron-Martin theorem (Lemma 3.2 in
van der Vaart and van Zanten (2008b)
) yields that
\tilde{\Pi}_{t,n,\theta,\mathcal{B}}\ll\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}
with Radon-Nikodym derivative

\displaystyle\left(\frac{d\tilde{\Pi}_{t,n,\theta,\mathcal{B}}}{d\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}}\right)(B)=\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}G_{n,t,\theta}^{*}(B)-\frac{1}{2n}t^{\prime}H_{n,\theta}^{*}t\right),

where I recall from Step 1 that
H_{n,\theta}^{*}
is a
d_{\gamma}\times d_{\gamma}
positive definite matrix with elements
H_{jk,n,\theta}^{*}=\langle\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta,j}^{*},\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta,k}^{*}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}
and
G_{n,t,\theta}^{*}(B)
is a random variable that satisfies
G_{n,t,\theta}^{*}(B)\sim\mathcal{N}(0,t^{\prime}H_{n,\theta}^{*}t)
under
\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}
. I argue that
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}t^{\prime}H_{n,\theta}^{*}t\leq C||t||_{2}^{2}n\zeta_{n}^{2}
for some constant. Applying the triangle inequality and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality I obtain,

\displaystyle t^{\prime}H_{n,\theta}^{*}t

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{d_{\gamma}}\sum_{k=1}^{d_{\gamma}}|t_{j}|\cdot|t_{k}|\cdot|\langle\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta,j}^{*},\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta,k}^{*}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{d_{\gamma}}\sum_{k=1}^{d_{\gamma}}|t_{j}|\cdot|t_{k}|\cdot||\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta,j}^{*}||_{\mathcal{H}}\cdot||\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta,k}^{*}||_{\mathcal{H}}

Since
\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta,j}^{*}=\sum_{l=1}^{d_{\gamma}}a_{0,jl}\tilde{g}_{n,\theta,l}^{*}
with
a_{0,jl}
being the
(j,l)
th entry of
V_{0}^{-1}
, it follows from the linearity of inner products and that

\displaystyle||\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta,j}^{*}||_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=\left|\sum_{l=1}^{d_{\gamma}}\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{d_{\gamma}}a_{0,jl}a_{0,jl^{\prime}}\langle\tilde{g}_{n,\theta,l}^{*},\tilde{g}_{n,\theta,l^{\prime}}^{*}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\right|\leq C_{j}\left(\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\max_{1\leq l\leq d_{\gamma}}||g_{n,\theta,l}^{*}||_{\mathcal{H}}\right)^{2},

where
C_{j}=\sum_{l=1}^{d_{\gamma}}a_{0,jl}^{2}
. Taking the maximum and applying Hölder’s inequality, it follows that there exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle t^{\prime}H_{n,\theta}^{*}t\leq C||t||^{2}_{2}\left(\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\max_{1\leq l\leq d_{\gamma}}||g_{n,\theta,l}^{*}||_{\mathcal{H}}\right)^{2}\lesssim||t||_{2}^{2}n\zeta_{n}^{2}

with the last inequality holding by assumption. Consequently, on the event
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
, I know that

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}G_{n,t,\theta}^{*}(B)\right|\lesssim\delta_{n}\sqrt{t^{\prime}H_{n,\theta}^{*}t}\lesssim||t||_{2}\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}\zeta_{n}

and

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\frac{1}{2n}t^{\prime}H_{n,\theta}^{*}t\lesssim\zeta_{n}^{2}.

This implies that, for any
t
in a neighborhood of zero,

\displaystyle\sup_{(\theta,B)\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\log\left(\frac{d\tilde{\Pi}_{t,n,\theta,\mathcal{B}}}{d\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}}\right)(B)\right|\leq\tilde{C}_{1}||t||_{2}\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}\zeta_{n}+\tilde{C}_{2}\zeta_{n}^{2}||t||_{2}^{2}

for some universal constants
\tilde{C}_{1},\tilde{C}_{2}>0
. Using that
\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}\zeta_{n}\rightarrow 0
and
\zeta_{n}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
(by assumption), I conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\theta,B)\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\log\left(\frac{d\tilde{\Pi}_{t,n,\theta,\mathcal{B}}}{d\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}}\right)(B)\right|\longrightarrow 0

as
n\rightarrow\infty
, and, as a result, I can conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{\int_{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}L_{n}(p_{t,n,\theta,B})d\Pi(\theta,B)}{\int_{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}L_{n}(p_{\theta,B})d\Pi(\theta,B)}

\displaystyle=(1+o_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}(1))

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{\int_{\Theta}\prod_{i=1}^{n}f_{\theta}(W_{i}|z_{i})\int\delta_{\theta,v+t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n}}(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z})\prod_{i=1}^{n}C(F_{\theta,z_{i}}(W_{i}),z_{i},\upsilon)d\tilde{\Pi}_{t,n,\theta,\mathcal{B}}(\upsilon)\pi_{\Theta}(\theta)d\theta}{\int_{\Theta}\prod_{i=1}^{n}f_{\theta}(W_{i}|z_{i})\int\delta_{\theta,B}(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z})\prod_{i=1}^{n}C(F_{\theta,z_{i}}(W_{i}),z_{i},B)d\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(B)\pi_{\Theta}(\theta)d\theta}

\displaystyle=(1+o_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}(1))

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{\int_{\Theta}\prod_{i=1}^{n}f_{\theta}(W_{i}|z_{i})\int\delta_{\theta,v+t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n}}(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z})\prod_{i=1}^{n}C(F_{\theta,z_{i}}(W_{i}),z_{i},\upsilon)d\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(\upsilon)\pi_{\Theta}(\theta)d\theta}{\int_{\Theta}\prod_{i=1}^{n}f_{\theta}(W_{i}|z_{i})\int\delta_{\theta,B}(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z})\prod_{i=1}^{n}C(F_{\theta,z_{i}}(W_{i}),z_{i},B)d\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}(B)\pi_{\Theta}(\theta)d\theta}

\displaystyle=(1+o_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}(1))\frac{\int_{\{(\theta,B):(\theta,B+t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n})\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}}L_{n}(p_{\theta,B})d\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}(B)\pi_{\Theta}(\theta)d\theta}{\int_{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}L_{n}(p_{\theta,B})d\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}(B)\pi_{\Theta}(\theta)d\theta}

\displaystyle=(1+o_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}(1))\frac{\Pi\left((\theta,B):(\theta,B+t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n})\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\middle|W^{(n)}\right)}{\Pi\left(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\middle|W^{(n)}\right)},

where the last equality holds by dividing and multiplying by
\int L_{n}(p_{\theta,B})d\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}(B)\pi_{\Theta}(\theta)d\theta
and applying the definition of the posterior.

Step 4.

It remains to show that

\displaystyle\frac{\Pi\left((\theta,B):(\theta,B+t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n})\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\middle|W^{(n)}\right)}{\Pi\left(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\middle|W^{(n)}\right)}=1+o_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}(1)

Since I established that

\displaystyle\Pi\left(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\middle|W^{(n)}\right)=1+o_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}(1)

in Step 1, I need to show that

\displaystyle\Pi\left((\theta,B):(\theta,B+t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n})\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\middle|W^{(n)}\right)=1+o_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}(1)

to conclude the result. Using the definition of
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\Pi\left((\theta,B):(\theta,B+t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n})\notin\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\middle|W^{(n)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pi\left((\theta,B):(\theta,B+t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n})\notin\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,1,W|Z}\middle|W^{(n)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\Pi\left((\theta,B):(\theta,B+t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n})\notin\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,2,W|Z}\middle|W^{(n)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pi\left(||B+t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n}||_{a}>D\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}\middle|W^{(n)}\right)+\Pi\left(h_{n,2}(p_{\theta,B+t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n}},p_{0,W|Z})\geq D\delta_{n}\middle|W^{(n)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\Pi\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}|G_{n,t,\theta}^{*}(B+t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n})|>L\delta_{n}\sqrt{t^{\prime}H_{n,\theta}^{*}t}\middle|W^{(n)}\right)

I show these probabilities converges to zero in
P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}
-probability in the following substeps.

Step 4A.

For the first probability, Lemma 8.1 of
van der Vaart and van Zanten (2008b)
implies that I can view
B
as a Gaussian random variable in
(C^{a}([0,1]^{d},\mathbb{R}),||\cdot||_{a})
without changing the RKHS
(\mathcal{H},\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\mathcal{H}})
because
C([0,1]^{d},\mathbb{R})
is the completion of
C^{a}([0,1]^{d},\mathbb{R})
with respect to the uniform norm
||\cdot||_{\infty}
and
||\cdot||_{\infty}\leq||\cdot||_{a}
. Since the RKHS norm is stronger than the Banach space norm, it follows
||t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}||_{a}/\sqrt{n}\lesssim\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}||t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}||_{\mathcal{H}}/\sqrt{n}
, and, as a result, I deduce that
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}||t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}||_{a}/\sqrt{n}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
because of the assumption on the RKHS norm of
\tilde{g}_{n,\theta}^{*}
. This means that the event
\{||B+t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n}||_{a}>D\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}\}
hardly differs from
\left\{||B||_{a}>D\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}\right\}
, and, as a result, the same argument as encountered in Step 1 of Proposition 2 leads to
\Pi(||B+t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n}||_{a}>D\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}|W^{(n)})\rightarrow 0
in
P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}
-probability as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Step 4B.

For the second probability, I use the triangle inequality to conclude that

\displaystyle h\left(p_{\theta,B+t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n}}(\cdot|z_{i}),p_{0,W|Z}(\cdot|z_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\leq h\left(p_{\theta,B+t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n}}(\cdot|z_{i}),p_{\theta,B}(\cdot|z_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+h\left(p_{\theta,B}(\cdot|z_{i}),p_{0,W|Z}(\cdot|z_{i})\right)

Applying Lemma 3.1 of
van der Vaart and van Zanten (2008a)
and adding/subtracting
t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}
inside
||\cdot||_{\infty}
, I know that

\displaystyle h\left(p_{\theta,B+t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n}}(\cdot|z_{i}),p_{\theta,B}(\cdot|z_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}||t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}||_{\infty}\exp\left(||t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}||_{\infty}/2\sqrt{n}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}||t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}-t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}||_{\infty}\exp\left(||t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}||_{\infty}/2\sqrt{n}\right)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}||t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}||_{\infty}\exp\left(||t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}||_{\infty}/2\sqrt{n}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}||t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}-t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}||_{\infty}\exp\left(||t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}-t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}||_{\infty}/2\sqrt{n}\right)\exp\left(||t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}||_{\infty}/2\sqrt{n}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}||t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}||_{\infty}\exp\left(||t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}-t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}||_{\infty}/2\sqrt{n}\right)\exp\left(||t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}||_{\infty}/2\sqrt{n}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=o(1)+(1+o(1))\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}||t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}||_{\infty}

for
i=1,...,n
, where the last equality holds because of the assumption that
\{\tilde{g}_{n,\theta,j}\}_{n\geq 1}
satisfies
\max_{1\leq j\leq d_{\gamma}}||\tilde{g}_{n,\theta,j}-\tilde{g}_{0,\theta,j}||_{\infty}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Since the assumptions imply
\{\tilde{\chi}_{0,\theta}:\theta\in\Theta\}
forms a uniformly bounded class of functions, there exists a constant
C\in(0,\infty)
such that

\displaystyle\Pi\left(h_{n,2}(p_{\theta,B+t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n}},p_{0,W|Z})\geq D\delta_{n}\middle|W^{(n)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\Pi\left(h_{n}\left(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z}\right)\geq D\delta_{n}-C/\sqrt{n}\middle|W^{(n)}\right).

for any
t
in a neighborhood of zero. Proposition 1 then implies that

\displaystyle\Pi\left(h_{n}\left(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z}\right)\geq D\delta_{n}-C/\sqrt{n}\middle|W^{(n)}\right)=o_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}(1)

as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Step 4C.

For the third probability, I apply Lemma 13 of
Ray and van der Vaart (2020)
to conclude that

\displaystyle G_{n,t,\theta}^{*}(B+t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n})\sim\mathcal{N}\left(-\frac{t^{\prime}H_{n,\theta}^{*}t}{\sqrt{n}},t^{\prime}H_{n,\theta}^{*}t\right)

under
\tilde{\Pi}_{\mathcal{B}}
. Since the mean
t^{\prime}H_{n,\theta}^{*}t/\sqrt{n}
is negligible relative to the standard deviation, a similar application of the Gaussian tail bound to that encountered Step 1 reveals that

\displaystyle\Pi\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}|G_{n,t,\theta}^{*}(B+t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{n,\theta}^{*}/\sqrt{n})|\geq D\delta_{n}\sqrt{t^{\prime}H_{n,\theta}^{*}t}\middle|W^{(n)}\right)=o_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}(1).

∎

3.2.
Proof of Proposition 4

Proposition 4
.

If Assumptions
15
and
16
hold, then Assumption
3
holds.

Proof.

The proof has two steps. Step 1 verifies that
q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})
exists, is unique, and is a measurable function of
p_{W|Z}
. Step 2 verifies that
\tilde{\gamma}\mapsto q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})
is a random contraction mapping and has a unique fixed point
\gamma_{n}
.

Step 1.

An
q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma
such that
Q_{n}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),p_{W|Z})=\inf_{\gamma\in\Gamma}Q_{n}(\gamma,p_{W|Z})
exists for each
p_{W|Z}
by Assumptions
15
.1 and
15
.3. Uniqueness of
q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})
for each
p_{W|Z}
holds by Assumption
15
.4. Notational adjustments to Lemma 2 of
Jennrich (1969)
establishes measurability of
p_{W|Z}\mapsto q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})
.

Step 2.

Let
F_{n}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})=2n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M(z_{i},\gamma)^{\prime}\Sigma(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})^{-1}m(z_{i},\gamma)
for every
(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\Gamma\times\mathcal{P}_{W|Z}
. Since
q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})
is an interior minimizer for every
p_{W|Z}\in\mathcal{P}_{W|Z}
, it follows that

\displaystyle F_{n}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})=0

for every
p_{W|Z}\in\mathcal{P}_{W|Z}
. The twice continuous differentiability of
\gamma\mapsto m(z,\gamma)
, the continuously differentiability of
\tilde{\gamma}\mapsto\Sigma(z,\tilde{\gamma})
, and the positive definiteness requirement for
\Sigma(z,\gamma)
enables use of the implicit function theorem to conclude that
(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma})\mapsto F_{n}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})
is continuously differentiable for each
p_{W|Z}\in\mathcal{P}_{W|Z}
with derivative equal to

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})=-\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{1}\partial x_{1}^{\prime}}Q_{n}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\right)^{-1}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{1}\partial x_{2}^{\prime}}Q_{n}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),

where it is recalled that
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
refer to the first two arguments of
Q_{n}
(i.e.,
Q_{n}(x_{1},x_{2},p_{W|Z})
). Consequently, the submultiplicativity of the operator norm implies

\displaystyle\left|\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\right|\right|_{op}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma}\left|\left|\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{1}\partial x_{1}^{\prime}}Q_{n}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\right)^{-1}\right|\right|_{op}

\displaystyle\quad\times\sup_{\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma}\left|\left|\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{1}\partial x_{2}^{\prime}}Q_{n}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\right|\right|_{op}

Since a Hessian matrix is real and symmetric,

\displaystyle\sup_{\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma}\left|\left|\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{1}\partial x_{1}^{\prime}}Q_{n}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\right)^{-1}\right|\right|_{op}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma}\lambda_{min}^{-1}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{1}\partial x_{1}^{\prime}}Q_{n}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left(\inf_{\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma}\lambda_{min}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{1}\partial x_{1}^{\prime}}Q_{n}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\right)\right)^{-1}

Consequently, Assumption
16
.4 implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma}\left|\left|\frac{\partial q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})}{\partial\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}\right|\right|_{op}<1

for each
p_{W|Z}\in\mathcal{P}_{W|Z}
. Coupled with the mean-value theorem, this implies that the continuous random operator
q(\cdot,p_{W|Z}):\Gamma\rightarrow\Gamma
is a random contraction operator. Theorem 7 of
Bharucha-Reid (1976)
then implies there is a random variable
\gamma_{n}
which is the unique fixed point of
q_{n}(\cdot,p_{W|Z})
.
∎

Supplementary Material (Not For Publication)

4.
Proofs of Lemmas

4.1.
Proofs of Lemmas Relevant to Theorem 1 and 2

Lemma 1
.

Suppose Assumptions
7
.1 and
10
.3 hold. Then, for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}=O(1),

where
\{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}_{n\geq 1}
are the sets introduced in Assumption
10
.

Proof.

First, note that Assumption and
7
.1
10
.3(c) imply that, for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\lambda_{max}(\Sigma(z_{i},\gamma))\leq 2\overline{\lambda}

and

\displaystyle\inf_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\min_{1\leq i\leq n}\lambda_{min}(\Sigma(z_{i},\gamma))\geq\frac{1}{2}\underline{\lambda}

for
n
large, by Weyl’s inequality. Consequently, for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
,
\{\{\Sigma(z_{i},\gamma)\}_{i=1}^{n}:(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}
forms a class of invertible matrices for
n
large. Next, by Assumptions
7
.1,
10
.3(c), and the submultiplicativity of the operator norm, I know that for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
,

(6)

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)(\Sigma(z_{i},\gamma)-\Sigma_{0}(z_{i},\gamma))||_{op}<\frac{1}{2}

for
n
large. Consequently, writing

\displaystyle\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)=\left(I+\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)(\Sigma(z_{i},\gamma)-\Sigma_{0}(z_{i},\gamma))\right)^{-1}\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma),

it follows that, for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
,

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}

\displaystyle\quad=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||(I+\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)(\Sigma(z_{i},\gamma)-\Sigma_{0}(z_{i},\gamma)))^{-1}\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}\cdot\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||(I+\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)(\Sigma(z_{i},\gamma)-\Sigma_{0}(z_{i},\gamma)))^{-1}||_{op}

\displaystyle\quad=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}\cdot\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\left|\left|\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}(\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)(\Sigma(z_{i},\gamma)-\Sigma_{0}(z_{i},\gamma)))^{k}\right|\right|_{op}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}\cdot\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}||(\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)(\Sigma(z_{i},\gamma)-\Sigma_{0}(z_{i},\gamma))||^{k}_{op}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op},

for
n
large and for all
(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
, where the first inequality uses the submultiplicativity of the operator norm, the second equality uses the Neumann series, the second inequality uses the triangle inequality (valid because the Neumann series is absolutely convergent), the third uses (
6
), and the fourth uses convergence of a geometric series. Consequently, by Assumption
7
.1, the following holds for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
:

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}\leq 2\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}\leq\frac{2}{\underline{\lambda}}<\infty

for
n
large.
∎

Lemma 2
.

Suppose that Assumptions
4
,
5
,
6
.1,
7
.1,
8
,
10
.1, and
10
.3 hold. Then, for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\mathcal{P}_{n,W|Z}}||q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-\gamma_{0}||_{2}=0

where
\{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}_{n\geq 1}
are the sets defined in Assumption
10
.

Proof.

The proof has three steps. Step 1 obtains a preliminary uniform convergence result. Step 2 uses Step 1 to establish a convergence result for the population objective function. Finally, Step 3 uses the result from Step 2 to conclude the lemma.

Step 1.

Let
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
be a fixed realization of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
. I show that

(7)

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}|Q_{n}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})|=0,

where
Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})=E_{P_{0,Z}}[m_{0}(Z,\gamma)^{\prime}\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(Z,\tilde{\gamma})m_{0}(Z,\gamma)]
. Since the triangle inequality implies that

\displaystyle|Q_{n}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})|

\displaystyle\leq|Q_{n}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-Q_{n}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})|

\displaystyle\quad+|Q_{n}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})-Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})|,

it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}|Q_{n}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-Q_{n}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})|=0

because Assumption
8
.1 implies

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma})\in\Gamma\times\Gamma}|Q_{n}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})-Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})|=0

Adding and subtracting
m_{0}(z_{i},\gamma)
and
\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})
, applying the triangle inequality, the operator norm inequality, and Hölder’s inequality, I obtain the upper bound

\displaystyle\left|Q_{n}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-Q_{n}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq||m(\cdot,\gamma)-m_{0}(\cdot,\gamma)||_{n,2}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})||_{op}||m(\cdot,\gamma)||_{n,2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+||m_{0}(\cdot,\gamma)||_{n,2}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})-\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})||_{op}||m(\cdot,\gamma)||_{n,2}

(8)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+||m_{0}(\cdot,\gamma)||_{n,2}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})||_{op}||m(\cdot,\gamma)-m_{0}(\cdot,\gamma)||_{n,2}

for all
n\geq 1
. Assumptions
6
.1 and
10
.3(a) implies

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||m(\cdot,\gamma)-m_{0}(\cdot,\gamma)||_{n,2}

\displaystyle=o(1),

\displaystyle\sup_{\gamma\in\Gamma}||m_{0}(\cdot,\gamma)||_{n,2}

\displaystyle=O(1),

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||m(\cdot,\gamma)||_{n,2}

\displaystyle=O(1),

where the third is an implication of the first two (by the triangle inequality). Lemma 1 shows that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}=O(1)

while Assumption
7
.1 implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}=O(1).

Consequently, I need to show that

(9)

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)-\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}=o(1)

to conclude from (
8
) that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}|Q_{n}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-Q_{n}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})|=o(1)

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. To show (
9
), I use the matrix identity
B^{-1}-A^{-1}=A^{-1}(A-B)B^{-1}
and the submultiplicativity of the operator norm to conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)-\Sigma^{-1}_{0}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}\times\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}_{0}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma(z_{i},\gamma)-\Sigma_{0}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}

\displaystyle\quad=O(1)O(1)o(1)

\displaystyle\quad=o(1)

where the first equality is Lemma 1, Assumption
7
, and Assumption
10
.3(c), respectively. Consequently, I can conclude that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}|Q_{n}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-Q_{n}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})|=0.

The result holds for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
because I fixed the realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
arbitrarily.

Step 2.

Let
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
be a fixed realization of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
. I show that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|Q_{P_{0,Z}}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})-Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma_{0},\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})\right|=o(1).

Since the criterion function satisfies

\displaystyle Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma_{0},\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})\leq Q_{P_{0,Z}}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})

for all
n
(as Assumption
5
implies
Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma_{0},\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})=0
), it suffices to show

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left(Q_{P_{0,Z}}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})-Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma_{0},\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})\right)=o(1),

To that end, I add/subtract
Q_{n}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})
to obtain

\displaystyle Q_{P_{0,Z}}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})-Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma_{0},\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})

\displaystyle\quad=Q_{P_{0,Z}}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})-Q_{n}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+Q_{n}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma_{0},\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})

\displaystyle\quad\leq Q_{P_{0,Z}}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})-Q_{n}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+Q_{n}(\gamma_{0},\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma_{0},\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z}),

where the inequality uses that
Q_{n}(\gamma_{0},\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\geq Q_{n}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})
, which holds because
q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma
minimizes
Q_{n}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})
. Using the definition of the supremum,

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left(Q_{P_{0,Z}}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})-Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma_{0},\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\sup_{(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|Q_{n}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})\right|

Applying Step 1, it then follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left(Q_{P_{0,Z}}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})-Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma_{0},\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})\right)=o(1)

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Since I fixed
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
arbitrarily, I can extend the argument so that it holds for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
.
