Step 3.

Let
A
be the set of
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
for which the claim of the lemma is not true, and assume by contradiction that
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}(A)>0
. For
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}\in A
, there is a constant
\xi>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-\gamma_{0}||_{2}\geq 2\xi.

for
n
large. Using the definition of the supremum, there exists
(\tilde{\gamma}_{n},p_{n,W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
such that
||q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma}_{n},p_{n,W|Z})-\gamma_{0}||_{2}\geq\xi
for
n
large. Using that

\displaystyle\inf_{\gamma\in\Gamma:||\gamma-\gamma_{0}||_{2}\geq\xi}Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma}_{n},p_{0,W|Z})\leq Q_{P_{0,Z}}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma}_{n},p_{n,W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma}_{n},p_{0,W|Z})

by definition of the infimum, it follows that

\displaystyle\inf_{\gamma\in\Gamma:||\gamma-\gamma_{0}||_{2}\geq\xi}Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma}_{n},p_{0,W|Z})-Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma_{0},\tilde{\gamma}_{n},p_{0,W|Z})

\displaystyle\quad\leq Q_{P_{0,Z}}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma}_{n},p_{n,W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma}_{n},p_{0,W|Z})-Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma_{0},\tilde{\gamma}_{n},p_{0,W|Z})

for
n
large. Step 2 shows that, for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left(Q_{P_{0,Z}}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})-Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma_{0},\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})\right)=o(1).

Consequently, the inequality

\displaystyle Q_{P_{0,Z}}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma}_{n},p_{n,W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma}_{n},p_{0,W|Z})-Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma_{0},\tilde{\gamma}_{n},p_{0,W|Z})

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left(Q_{P_{0,Z}}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})-Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma_{0},\tilde{\gamma},p_{0,W|Z})\right)

implies

\displaystyle Q_{P_{0,Z}}(q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma}_{n},p_{n,W|Z}),\tilde{\gamma}_{n},p_{0,W|Z})-Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma_{0},\tilde{\gamma}_{n},p_{0,W|Z})=o(1).

Meanwhile, Assumption
5
.2, Assumption
7
.1, and Assumption
8
.2 implies that

\displaystyle\inf_{\gamma\in\Gamma:||\gamma-\gamma_{0}||_{2}\geq\xi}Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma}_{n},p_{0,W|Z})-Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma_{0},\tilde{\gamma}_{n},p_{0,W|Z})

\displaystyle\quad=\inf_{\gamma\in\Gamma:||\gamma-\gamma_{0}||_{2}\geq\xi}Q_{P_{0,Z}}(\gamma,\tilde{\gamma}_{n},p_{0,W|Z})

\displaystyle\quad\geq\inf_{(z,\gamma)\in\mathcal{Z}\times\Gamma}\lambda_{min}(\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z,\gamma))\inf_{\gamma\in\Gamma:||\gamma-\gamma_{0}||_{2}\geq\xi}E_{P_{0}}[||m_{0}(Z,\gamma)||_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle\quad\geq\frac{1}{\overline{\lambda}}\inf_{\gamma\in\Gamma:||\gamma-\gamma_{0}||_{2}\geq\xi}E_{P_{0}}[||m_{0}(Z,\gamma)||_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle\quad>\varepsilon

for some
\varepsilon>0
(depending on
\xi
and
\overline{\lambda}
). This leads to a contradiction. Consequently,
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}(A)=0
and the proof is complete.
∎

Lemma 3
.

If Assumptions
4
,
5
,
6
,
7
.1,
8
,
9
, and
10
hold, then, for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
and
n
large, the function
\tilde{\gamma}\mapsto q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})
is a uniform contraction mapping over
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
. That is, there exists a constant
\rho\in[0,1)
and
N\geq 1
such that
||q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma}_{1},p_{W|Z})-q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma}_{2},p_{W|Z})||_{2}\leq\rho||\tilde{\gamma}_{1}-\tilde{\gamma}_{2}||_{2}
for all
\tilde{\gamma}_{1},\tilde{\gamma}_{2}\in\Gamma
, all
p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
, and
n\geq N
.

Proof.

The proof has three steps. Step 1 establishes some intermediate technical results. Step 2 establishes the claim.

Step 1.

I start by showing some intermediate technical results. Let

\displaystyle B_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\bigg\{\left[m(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))^{\prime}\otimes M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))^{\prime}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\times\left[\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})\otimes\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})\right]\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}\text{vec}(\Sigma(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma}))\bigg\}

and

\displaystyle D_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))^{\prime}\Sigma(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})^{-1}M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))^{\prime}\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})\otimes I)\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma^{\prime}}\text{vec}(M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))^{\prime}).

Steps 1A. and 1B-C. show 1.
\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|B_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\right|\right|_{op}=o(1)
, and 2.
D_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})
is invertible for large
n
and
\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|D_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})^{-1}\right|\right|_{op}=O(1)
, respectively.

Step 1A.

I show that
\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|B_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\right|\right|_{op}=o(1).
Using the submultiplicativity of the operator norm and the fact that
||A\otimes B||_{op}=||A||_{op}\cdot||B||_{op}
for matrices
A
and
B
,

\displaystyle\left|\left|B_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\right|\right|_{op}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})||_{op}\right)^{2}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\left|\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}\text{vec}(\Sigma(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma}))\right|\right|_{op}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{||m(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))||_{op}\cdot||M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))||_{op}\right\}

Lemma 1 implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})||_{op}=O(1)

while Assumption
10
.3(e) implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\left|\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}\text{vec}(\Sigma(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma}))\right|\right|_{op}=O(1).

Consequently, I need to show that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{||m(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))||_{op}\cdot||M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))||_{op}\right\}=o(1)

Applying Hölder’s inequality, I conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{||m(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))||_{op}\cdot||M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))||_{op}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||m(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))||_{2}^{2}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))||_{op}^{2}}.

The triangle inequality and a mean-value expansion reveals that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||m(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))||_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||m(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))-m_{0}(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))||_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||m_{0}(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))||_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||m(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))-m_{0}(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))||_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+||q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-\gamma_{0}||_{2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||M_{0}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{*})||_{op}^{2}},

where
\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{*}
is on the line segment joining
q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})
and
\gamma_{0}
(and takes different values for each row of
M_{0}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{*})
). Such an expansion is valid because, for large
n
, Lemma 2 implies that
q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}
for all
\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma
and all
p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
, and, as a result, I can apply Assumption
6
.2 to obtain the mean-value expansion. Lemma 2 and Assumption
6
.2 implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left(||q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-\gamma_{0}||_{2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||M_{0}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma}_{n}^{*})||_{op}^{2}}\right)=o(1)

while

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||m(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))-m_{0}(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))||_{2}^{2}}=o(1)

by Assumption
10
.3(a). Consequently,

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||m(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))||_{2}^{2}}=o(1).

Lemma 2 implies that
\{q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}):(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}\subseteq\Gamma_{0}
for large
n
, where
\Gamma_{0}
is the neighborhood of
\gamma_{0}
defined in Assumption
6
.2. Consequently, the triangle inequality and this observation imply that, for
n
large,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))||_{op}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))-M_{0}(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))||_{op}^{2}}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||M_{0}(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))||_{op}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||M(z_{i},\gamma)-M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}^{2}}+\sup_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}^{2}},

for all
\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma_{0}
. Consequently, it follows that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))||_{op}^{2}}\leq\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}^{2}}<\infty

using Assumption
6
.2 and Assumption
10
.3(b). Putting these two results together,

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{||m(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))||_{op}\cdot||M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))||_{op}\right\}=o(1).

This leads to the conclusion that
\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||B_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})||_{op}=o(1)
.

Step 1B.

I show that

(10)

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|D_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-E_{P_{0,Z}}[M_{0}(Z,\gamma_{0})^{\prime}\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(Z,\tilde{\gamma})M_{0}(Z,\gamma_{0})]\right|\right|_{op}=o(1)

and then, in Step 1C, I show that this implies
\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||D_{n}^{-1}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})||_{op}=O(1)
. Consider the first term in the expression for
D_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))^{\prime}\Sigma(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})^{-1}M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})).

The triangle inequality, Hölder’s inequality, Lemma 1, and Assumption
10
.3(b)-
10
.3(c) implies that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))^{\prime}\Sigma(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})^{-1}M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{0}(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))^{\prime}\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})M_{0}(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))+o(1)

uniformly over
\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
. Next, I show that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{0}(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))^{\prime}\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})M_{0}(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))

(11)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})^{\prime}\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})+o(1)

uniformly over
\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
. Applying the triangle inequality and Hölder’s inequality, I obtain the following inequality

\displaystyle\left|\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(M_{0}(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))-M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})\right)^{\prime}\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})M_{0}(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))\right|\right|_{op}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})||_{op}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|M_{0}(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))-M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})\right|\right|_{op}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|M_{0}(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))\right|\right|_{op}^{2}}.

Assumption
7
.1 implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma_{0}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})||_{op}=O(1).

Using Assumption
6
.2 and that Lemma 2 implies
\{q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}):(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}\subseteq\Gamma_{0}
for large
n
, I can conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|M_{0}(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))\right|\right|_{op}^{2}}\leq\sup_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma)\right|\right|_{op}^{2}}=O(1).

for large
n
, and, as a result, I obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|M_{0}(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))\right|\right|_{op}^{2}}=O(1).

Assumption
6
.2 and Lemma 2 implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|M_{0}(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))-M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})\right|\right|_{op}^{2}}=o(1).

by the mean-value theorem. Consequently,

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(M_{0}(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))-M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})\right)^{\prime}\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})M_{0}(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))\right|\right|_{op}=o(1).

A similar argument reveals that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})^{\prime}\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})\left[M_{0}(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))-M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})\right]\right|\right|_{op}=o(1).

As such, I am able to conclude (
11
). Applying Assumption
9
.1, I can further conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})^{\prime}\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})=E_{P_{0,Z}}[M_{0}(Z,\gamma_{0})^{\prime}\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(Z,\tilde{\gamma})M_{0}(Z,\gamma_{0})]+o(1)

uniformly over
\Gamma_{0}
. It remains to show that

(12)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))^{\prime}\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})\otimes I)\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma^{\prime}}\text{vec}(M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))^{\prime})=o(1)

uniformly over
\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
to conclude (
10
). I use the triangle inequality, submultiplicativity of operator norms, and Hölder’s inequality to conclude that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))^{\prime}\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})\otimes I)\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma^{\prime}}\text{vec}(M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))^{\prime})\right|\right|_{op}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|(m(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))^{\prime}\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})\otimes I)\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma^{\prime}}\text{vec}(M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))^{\prime})\right|\right|_{op}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|(m(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))\right|\right|_{op}\left|\left|\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})\right|\right|_{op}\left|\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma^{\prime}}\text{vec}(M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))^{\prime})\right|\right|_{op}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})||_{op}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|m(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))\right|\right|_{op}\cdot\left|\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma^{\prime}}\text{vec}(M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))^{\prime})\right|\right|_{op}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})||_{op}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||m(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))||_{2}^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma^{\prime}}\text{vec}(M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))^{\prime})\right|\right|_{F}^{2}}.

Step 1A shows that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||m(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))||_{2}^{2}}=o(1).

Lemma 1 implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})||_{op}=O(1).

Since Lemma 2 implies
\{q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}):(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}\subseteq\Gamma_{0}
for large
n
, Assumption
10
.3(d) implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma^{\prime}}\text{vec}(M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))^{\prime})\right|\right|_{F}^{2}}=O(1)

This leads to the conclusion that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))^{\prime}\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\tilde{\gamma})\otimes I)\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma^{\prime}}\text{vec}(M(z_{i},q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))^{\prime})=o(1)

uniformly over
\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
, and, as a result, I am able to conclude (
12
).

Step 1C.

Let
V_{0}(\tilde{\gamma})=E_{P_{0,Z}}[M_{0}(Z,\gamma_{0})^{\prime}\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(Z,\tilde{\gamma})M_{0}(Z,\gamma_{0})]
, and note that, by Assumptions
7
.1 and
9
.2,
\{V_{0}(\tilde{\gamma}):\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}\}
forms a bounded class of matrices. The convergence (
10
) implies that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}|\det(D_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))-\det(V_{0}(\tilde{\gamma}))|=0

Consequently, for every
\xi>0
, there exists
N_{\varepsilon}\geq 1
such that

\displaystyle\inf_{\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma_{0}}\det(V_{0}(\tilde{\gamma})-\xi<\inf_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\det(D_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))

and

\displaystyle\sup_{\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma_{0}}\det(V_{0}(\tilde{\gamma}))+\xi>\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\det(D_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))

for all
n\geq N_{\xi}
. Since Assumptions
7
.1 and
9
.2 imply that the LHS of the above inequalities is contained in
(0,\infty)
for
\xi>0
small and for
n
large, the class
\{D_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}):(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}
is invertible for large
n
. Using the identity

\displaystyle D_{n}^{-1}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})=(I+V_{0}^{-1}(\tilde{\gamma})(D_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-V_{0}(\tilde{\gamma})))^{-1}V_{0}^{-1}(\tilde{\gamma})

and the fact that Assumption
7
.1, Assumption
9
.2, and (
10
) implies

(13)

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||V_{0}^{-1}(\tilde{\gamma})(D_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-V_{0}(\tilde{\gamma}))||_{op}<\frac{1}{2}

for large
n
, it follows that, for large
n
,

\displaystyle||D_{n}^{-1}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})||_{op}

\displaystyle\leq||(I+V_{0}^{-1}(\tilde{\gamma})(D_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-V_{0}(\tilde{\gamma})))^{-1}||_{op}\cdot||V_{0}^{-1}(\tilde{\gamma})||_{op}

\displaystyle=\left|\left|\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}V_{0}^{-1}(\tilde{\gamma})(D_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-V_{0}(\tilde{\gamma}))^{k}\right|\right|_{op}\cdot||V_{0}^{-1}(\tilde{\gamma})||_{op}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma_{0}}||V_{0}^{-1}(\tilde{\gamma})||_{op}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left|\left|V_{0}^{-1}(\tilde{\gamma})(D_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-V_{0}(\tilde{\gamma}))\right|\right|_{op}^{k}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma_{0}}||V_{0}^{-1}(\tilde{\gamma})||_{op}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{-k}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma_{0}}||V_{0}^{-1}(\tilde{\gamma})||_{op}<\infty

holds uniformly over
\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
, where the first inequality uses the submultiplicativity of the operator norm, the equality uses the Neumann series, the second inequality is the triangle inequality and the submultiplicativity of the operator norm, the third inequality uses (
13
), and the fourth inequality convergence of a geometric series and the fact that Assumptions
7
.1 and
9
.2 imply
\sup_{\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma_{0}}||V_{0}^{-1}(\tilde{\gamma})||_{op}<\infty
. Consequently, I conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||D_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})||_{op}=O(1).

Step 2.

I use the results from Step 1 to establish that
q_{n}(\cdot,p_{W|Z}):\Gamma_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d_{\gamma}}
is a uniform contraction mapping over
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
. Applying the Implicit Function Theorem,
\tilde{\gamma}\mapsto q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})
is continuously differentiable over
\Gamma_{0}
for large
n
with derivative given by

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})=-D_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})^{-1}B_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}),

Consequently, by the Mean-Value Theorem,

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma}_{1},p_{W|Z})-q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma}_{2},p_{W|Z})||_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|D_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})^{-1}B_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\right|\right|_{op}\cdot||\tilde{\gamma}_{1}-\tilde{\gamma}_{2}||_{2}.

for any
\tilde{\gamma}_{1},\tilde{\gamma}_{2}\in\Gamma_{0}
. Using the submultiplicativity of the operator norm to conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\right|\right|_{op}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|D_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})^{-1}\right|\right|_{op}

\displaystyle\quad\times\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|B_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\right|\right|_{op}

\displaystyle\quad=O(1)o(1)

\displaystyle\quad=o(1)

by Step 1. Consequently, there is a constant
\rho\in[0,1)
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|D_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})^{-1}B_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\right|\right|_{op}\leq\rho

for large
n
, establishing that
q_{n}(\cdot,p_{W|Z}):\Gamma_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d_{\gamma}}
is a uniform (over
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
) contraction for large
n
.
∎

Lemma 4
.

If Assumptions
3
,
4
,
5
,
6
,
7
.1,
8
,
9
, and
10
hold, then, for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\gamma_{n}-\gamma_{0}||_{2}=0,

where
\{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}_{n\geq 1}
are the sets defined in Assumption
10
.

Proof.

Let
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
be an arbitrary fixed realization of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
. I show that for any
\xi>0
, there exists a
N_{\xi}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\gamma_{n}-\gamma_{0}||_{2}<\xi

for all
n\geq N_{\xi}
. Let
\xi>0
be arbitrary. Recall that
\gamma_{n,r}
is defined by the recursive relation
\gamma_{n,r}=q_{n}(\gamma_{n,r-1},p_{W|Z})
for
r\geq 1
and some initial condition
\gamma_{n,0}\in\Gamma
. I first claim that there is a sequence
\{r_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
and
N_{\xi,1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}
such that

(14)

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\gamma_{n}-\gamma_{n,r_{n}}||_{2}<\frac{\xi}{2}

for all
n\geq N_{\xi,1}
. Without loss of generality I can set
\gamma_{n,0}=q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z})
for some
\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma
, since, for any given alternative
\tilde{\gamma}_{n,0}\in\Gamma
, one can always redefine
\gamma_{n,0}=q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma}_{n,0},p_{W|Z})
. Lemma 2 thus implies that
\{\gamma_{n,r}(p_{W|Z}):p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z},r\geq 0\}\subseteq\Gamma_{0}
for
n
large. Moreover, since Lemma 3 implies that
\{q_{n}(\cdot,p_{W|Z}):\Gamma_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d_{\gamma}},p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}
defines a collection of contraction mappings for
n
large with a common (across
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
) constant
\rho\in[0,1)
, I can use the a priori estimate of the contraction mapping to obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\gamma_{n}-\gamma_{n,r}||_{2}\leq\frac{\rho^{r}}{1-\rho}\cdot\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\gamma_{n,1}-\gamma_{n,0}||_{2}

Since
\rho\in[0,1)
and
\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\gamma_{n,1}-\gamma_{n,0}||_{2}=o(1)
by Lemma 2, I can find
\{r_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
with
r_{n}\rightarrow\infty
as
n\rightarrow\infty
such that (
14
) holds. Next, since
q_{n}(\gamma_{n,r_{n}-1},p_{W|Z})\in\{q_{n}(\tilde{\gamma},p_{W|Z}):\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma\}
, Lemma 2 implies there exists
N_{\xi,2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\gamma_{n,r_{n}}-\gamma_{0}||_{2}<\frac{\xi}{2}

for all
n\geq N_{\xi,2}
. Defining
N_{\xi}=\max\{N_{\xi,1},N_{\xi,2}\}
and applying the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\gamma_{n}-\gamma_{0}||_{2}<\xi

for all
n\geq N_{\xi}
. Since
\xi>0
was chosen arbitrarily, the claim follows.
∎

Lemma 5
.

Suppose that Assumptions
3
,
4
,
5
,
6
,
7
,
8
,
9
, and
10
hold. Then, for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|M(\cdot,\gamma_{n})-M_{0}(\cdot,\gamma_{0})\right|\right|_{n,2}\lesssim o(n^{-1/4})+\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\gamma_{n}-\gamma_{0}||_{2}

and

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|\Sigma(\cdot,\gamma_{n})-\Sigma_{0}(\cdot,\gamma_{0})\right|\right|_{n,\infty}\lesssim o(n^{-1/4})+\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\gamma_{n}-\gamma_{0}||_{2}

where
\{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}_{n\geq 1}
are the sets defined in Assumption
10
.

Proof.

The proof has two steps with each corresponding to the statements in the lemma. That is, Step 1 verifies the first claim and Step 2 verifies the second.

Step 1.

Let
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
be a fixed realization of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
. Since
\gamma_{n}\in\Gamma_{0}
for all
p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
and
n
large by Lemma 4, I can apply the triangle inequality and the definition of the supremum to conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|M(\cdot,\gamma_{n})-M_{0}(\cdot,\gamma_{0})\right|\right|_{n,2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|M(\cdot,\gamma_{n})-M_{0}(\cdot,\gamma_{n})\right|\right|_{n,2}+\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|M_{0}(\cdot,\gamma_{n})-M_{0}(\cdot,\gamma_{0})\right|\right|_{n,2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|M(\cdot,\gamma)-M_{0}(\cdot,\gamma)\right|\right|_{n,2}+\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|M_{0}(\cdot,\gamma_{n})-M_{0}(\cdot,\gamma_{0})\right|\right|_{n,2}.

for large
n
. Applying Assumption
10
.3(b), I know that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|M(\cdot,\gamma)-M_{0}(\cdot,\gamma)\right|\right|_{n,2}=o(n^{-\frac{1}{4}})

Since
\{\gamma_{n}(p_{W|Z}):p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}\subseteq\Gamma_{0}
for large
n
by Lemma 4, Assumption
6
.2 and the mean-value theorem implies there exists
\overline{M}_{0}\in[0,\infty)
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|M_{0}(\cdot,\gamma_{n})-M_{0}(\cdot,\gamma_{0})\right|\right|_{n,2}\leq\overline{M}_{0}\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\gamma_{n}-\gamma_{0}||_{2}

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Together, these results imply that the following holds

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|M(\cdot,\gamma_{n})-M_{0}(\cdot,\gamma_{0})\right|\right|_{n,2}\lesssim o(n^{-1/4})+\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\gamma_{n}-\gamma_{0}||_{2}

Since the argument holds for an arbitrary fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
, I can conclude that it holds for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
.

Step 2.

Let
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
be a fixed realization of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
. Applying the triangle inequality and the definition of the supremum, I know that

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\Sigma(\cdot,\gamma_{n})-\Sigma_{0}(\cdot,\gamma_{0})||_{n,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\Sigma(\cdot,\gamma_{n})-\Sigma_{0}(\cdot,\gamma_{n})||_{n,\infty}+\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\Sigma_{0}(\cdot,\gamma_{n})-\Sigma_{0}(\cdot,\gamma_{0})||_{n,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\Sigma(\cdot,\gamma)-\Sigma_{0}(\cdot,\gamma)||_{n,\infty}+\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\Sigma_{0}(\cdot,\gamma_{n})-\Sigma_{0}(\cdot,\gamma_{0})||_{n,\infty}.

Assumption
10
.3(c) yields

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\Sigma(\cdot,\gamma)-\Sigma_{0}(\cdot,\gamma)||_{n,\infty}=o(n^{-\frac{1}{4}})

Moreover, Lemma 4 implies that
\gamma_{n}\in\Gamma_{0}
for large
n
. Consequently, Assumption
7
.2 implies that there exists a constant
\overline{\Sigma}_{0}\in[0,\infty)
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\Sigma_{0}(\cdot,\gamma_{n})-\Sigma_{0}(\cdot,\gamma_{0})||_{n,\infty}\leq\overline{\Sigma}_{0}\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\gamma_{n}-\gamma_{0}||_{2}.

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Putting these results together,

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\Sigma(\cdot,\gamma_{n})-\Sigma_{0}(\cdot,\gamma_{0})||_{n,\infty}\lesssim o(n^{-1/4})+\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\gamma_{n}-\gamma_{0}||_{2}.

Since the argument holds for an arbitrary fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
, I can conclude that it holds for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
.
∎

Lemma 6
.

Let
V_{n}(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M(z_{i},\gamma)^{\prime}\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)M(z_{i},\bar{\gamma})
. If Assumptions
3
,
4
,
5
,
6
,
7
,
8
,
9
, and
10
hold, then, for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
and for every positive sequence
\{r_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
with
r_{n}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, the collection of matrices
\{V_{n}(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z}):(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in B(0,r_{n})\times B(0,r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}
forms a class of nonsingular matrices for
n
large, where
\{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}_{n\geq 1}
are defined in Assumption
10
and
\{B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\}_{n\geq 1}
are open balls in
(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\gamma}},||\cdot||_{2})
centered at
\gamma_{0}
and with radii
\{r_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
. Moreover,
\sup_{(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||V_{n}^{-1}(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})||_{op}=O(1)
.

Proof.

The proof has three steps. Step 1 establishes an intermediate uniform convergence property. Step 2 establishes the first claim. Step 3 establishes the second claim.

Step 1.

As an intermediate technical result, I show that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|V_{n}(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-V_{0}\right|\right|_{F}=0,

where
||\cdot||_{F}
denotes the Frobenius norm. Since Assumption
9
.1 implies that

||V_{n}(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{0},p_{0,W|Z})-V_{0}||_{F}=o(1),

it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|V_{n}(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-V_{n}(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{0},p_{0,W|Z})\right|\right|_{F}=0.

Adding and subtracting
M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})
and
\Sigma_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})
appropriately, I obtain

\displaystyle V_{n}(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-V_{n}(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{0},p_{0,W|Z})

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M(z_{i},\gamma)^{\prime}(\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)-\Sigma^{-1}_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0}))M(z_{i},\bar{\gamma})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M(z_{i},\gamma)^{\prime}\Sigma^{-1}_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})(M(z_{i},\bar{\gamma})-M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(M(z_{i},\gamma)-M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0}))^{\prime}\Sigma^{-1}_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})^{-1}M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})

for all
(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
. The triangle inequality then reveals that it suffices to show three results

(15)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|M(z_{i},\gamma)^{\prime}(\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)-\Sigma^{-1}_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0}))M(z_{i},\bar{\gamma})\right|\right|_{F}=o(1)

(16)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|M(z_{i},\gamma)^{\prime}\Sigma^{-1}_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})(M(z_{i},\bar{\gamma})-M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0}))\right|\right|_{F}=o(1)

(17)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|(M(z_{i},\gamma)-M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0}))^{\prime}\Sigma^{-1}_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})^{-1}M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})\right|\right|_{F}=o(1)

uniformly in
(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
. I verify these using three sub-steps.

Step 1A.

For (
15
), I use the submultiplicative property of the Frobenius norm and apply Hölder’s inequality to conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|M(z_{i},\gamma)^{\prime}(\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)-\Sigma^{-1}_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0}))M(z_{i},\bar{\gamma})\right|\right|_{F}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)-\Sigma^{-1}_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{F}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\left(\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|M(z_{i},\gamma)\right|\right|_{F}^{2}}\right)^{2}.

Using the matrix identity
A^{-1}-B^{-1}=B^{-1}(B-A)A^{-1}
and the submultiplicativity of the Frobenius norm, I know that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)-\Sigma^{-1}_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{F}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{F}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{F}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma(z_{i},\gamma)-\Sigma_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{F}.

Part 2 of Lemma 5 and
||\cdot||_{F}\leq\sqrt{d_{g}}||\cdot||_{op}
implies that

(18)

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma(z_{i},\gamma)-\Sigma_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{F}\lesssim\sup_{\gamma\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})}||\gamma-\gamma_{0}||_{2}+o(n^{-\frac{1}{4}})=o(1).

for
r_{n}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. The symmetry of
\Sigma^{-1}_{0}(z_{i},\gamma)
and Assumption
7
implies that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{F}

\displaystyle=\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{d_{g}}\lambda_{j}^{2}(\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{d_{g}}}{\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\min_{1\leq i\leq n}\lambda_{min}(\Sigma_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{d_{g}}}{\underline{\lambda}}<\infty.

Lemma 1 and the inequality
||A||_{F}\leq\sqrt{d_{g}}||A||_{op}
for any
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{g}\times d_{g}}
implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{F}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sqrt{d_{g}}\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}=O(1).

Consequently,

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)-\Sigma^{-1}_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{F}=o(1).

Applying the triangle inequality and Part 1 of Lemma 5, there is a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|M(z_{i},\gamma)\right|\right|_{F}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|M(z_{i},\gamma)-M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})\right|\right|_{F}^{2}}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})\right|\right|_{F}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\sup_{\gamma\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})}\left|\left|\gamma-\gamma_{0}\right|\right|_{2}+o(n^{-\frac{1}{4}})+\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})\right|\right|_{F}^{2}},

Consequently, I use that
r_{n}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
and Assumption
6
to conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|M(z_{i},\gamma)\right|\right|_{F}^{2}}=O(1).

Step 1B.

For (
16
), I use the submultiplicativity of the Frobenius norm and apply Hölder’s inequality to obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|M(z_{i},\gamma)^{\prime}\Sigma^{-1}_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})(M(z_{i},\bar{\gamma})-M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0}))\right|\right|_{F}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|M(z_{i},\gamma)\right|\right|_{F}^{2}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{F}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|M(z_{i},\gamma)-M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0}))\right|\right|_{F}^{2}}.

In dealing with (
15
), I showed that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|M(z_{i},\gamma)\right|\right|_{F}^{2}}=O(1).

Assumption
7
and the equivalence between the Frobenius and operator norm implies that

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{F}=O(1).

Part 1 of Lemma 5 shows that

(19)

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\times B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|M(z_{i},\gamma)-M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})\right|\right|_{F}^{2}}\lesssim\sup_{\gamma\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})}||\gamma-\gamma_{0}||_{2}+o(n^{-\frac{1}{4}}).

Since
r_{n}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, I conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|M(z_{i},\gamma)^{\prime}\Sigma^{-1}_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})(M(z_{i},\gamma)-M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0}))\right|\right|_{F}=o(1).

Step 1C.

For (
17
), a similar argument to that applied (
16
) to yields that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\left|(M(z_{i},\gamma)-M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0}))^{\prime}\Sigma^{-1}_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})^{-1}M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})\right|\right|_{F}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{F}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{F}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||M(z_{i},\gamma)-M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{F}^{2}}

(20)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{F}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{F}^{2}}\left(\sup_{\gamma\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})}||\gamma-\gamma_{0}||_{2}+o(n^{-\frac{1}{4}})\right)

\displaystyle\quad=o(1).

Step 2.

Since
V_{0}
is a bounded matrix (by Assumptions
7
.1 and
9
.2), Step 1 implies that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}|\det(V_{n}(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))-\det(V_{0})|=0

Consequently, for every
\xi>0
, there exists
N_{\xi}\geq 1
such that

\displaystyle\det(V_{0})-\xi<\inf_{(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\det(V_{n}(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))

and

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\det(V_{n}(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z}))<\det(V_{0})+\xi

for all
n\geq N_{\xi}
. Setting
\xi=\det(V_{0})/2>0
(by Assumption
7
.1 and
9
.2), it follows that

\displaystyle\{\det V_{n}(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z}):(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in B(0,r_{n})\times B(0,r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}\subseteq\left[\frac{\det(V_{0})}{2},\frac{3\det(V_{0})}{2}\right]

for
n
large. This establishes that
\{V_{n}(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z}):(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in B(0,r_{n})\times B(0,r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}
forms a class of nonsingular matrices for
n
large.

Step 3.

Step 1 implies
\sup_{(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||V_{n}(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-V_{0}||_{op}=o(1)
and Assumptions
7
.1 and
9
.2 imply that
||V_{0}^{-1}||_{op}<\infty
. Consequently, the submultiplicativity of operator norms implies

\sup_{(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||V_{0}^{-1}(V_{n}(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-V_{0})||_{op}<1/2

for
n
large. Consequently, I obtain, for
n
large,

\displaystyle||V_{n}^{-1}(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})||_{op}

\displaystyle=||(I+V_{0}^{-1}(V_{n}(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-V_{0}))^{-1}V_{0}^{-1}||_{op}

\displaystyle\leq||(I+V_{0}^{-1}(V_{n}(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-V_{0}))^{-1}||_{op}\cdot||V_{0}^{-1}||_{op}

\displaystyle=\left|\left|\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}(V_{0}^{-1}(V_{n}(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-V_{0}))^{k}\right|\right|_{op}\cdot||V_{0}^{-1}||_{op}

\displaystyle\leq||V_{0}^{-1}||_{op}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}||V_{0}^{-1}(V_{n}(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-V_{0})||_{op}^{k}

\displaystyle\leq||V_{0}^{-1}||_{op}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{k}

\displaystyle\leq 2||V_{0}^{-1}||_{op},

where the first equality identity
V_{n}^{-1}(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})=(I+V_{0}^{-1}(V_{n}(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-V_{0}))^{-1}V_{0}^{-1}
, the first inequality uses submultiplicativity of the operator norm, the second equality is the Neumann series, the second inequality is the triangle inequality, the third inequality is the uniform bound on
||V_{0}^{-1}(V_{n}(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})-V_{0})||_{op}
, and the final inequality uses the formula for geometric series. Consequently,

\sup_{(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||V_{n}^{-1}(\gamma,\bar{\gamma},p_{W|Z})||_{op}<\infty

for
n
large.
∎

Remark 1
.

If
r_{n}=o(n^{-1/4})
, then an inspection of the proof reveals that RHS of (
15
), (
16
), and (
17
) can be strengthened to
o(n^{-1/4})
(in particular, (
18
), (
19
), and (
20
) become
o(n^{-1/4})
). Since Lemma 7 establishes that
\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\gamma_{n}-\gamma_{0}||_{2}=o(n^{-1/4})
, one can assume that
\gamma_{n},\gamma_{n}^{\dagger}\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})
with
r_{n}=o(n^{-1/4})
for large
n
. Consequently, the three claims in Step 4 of the proof of Theorem 1 follow.

Lemma 7
.

Suppose that Assumptions
3
,
4
,
5
,
6
,
7
,
8
,
9
, and
10
.1–
10
.5 hold. Then, for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
, the following holds:

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||n^{\frac{1}{4}}(\gamma_{n}-\gamma_{0})||_{2}=0,

where
\{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}_{n\geq 1}
are the sets defined in Assumption
10
.

Proof.

The proof has three steps. Step 1 verifies the conclusion of the lemma after asserting two intermediate technical results. Step 2 and Step 3 are dedicated to the verification these two results.

Step 1.

Let
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
be an arbitrary fixed realization of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
. First note that
\gamma_{n}
satisfies first order conditions

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M(z_{i},\gamma_{n})^{\prime}\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma_{n})m(z_{i},\gamma_{n})=0.

Since
\gamma_{n}\in\Gamma_{0}
for large
n
, I expand
m(z_{i},\gamma_{n})
around
\gamma_{0}
(using element by element mean value expansions) to obtain

\displaystyle m(z_{i},\gamma_{n})=m(z_{i},\gamma_{0})+M(z_{i},\gamma_{n}^{\dagger})(\gamma_{n}-\gamma_{0}),

for all
i=1,...,n
, where
\{\gamma_{n}^{\dagger}\}_{n\geq 1}
is a sequence such that
\gamma_{n}^{\dagger}
on the line segment joining
\gamma_{n}
and
\gamma_{0}
(and takes different values in each row of
M(z_{i},\gamma_{n}^{\dagger})
). Substituting this into the first order conditions, I conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M(z_{i},\gamma_{n})^{\prime}\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma_{n})m(z_{i},\gamma_{0})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M(z_{i},\gamma_{n})^{\prime}\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma_{n})M(z_{i},\gamma_{n}^{\dagger})(\gamma_{n}-\gamma_{0})=0

for
n
large. Consequently, for
n
large, an application of Lemma 6 (valid because Lemma 4 implies
\gamma_{n},\gamma_{n}^{\dagger}\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})
for
n
large, where
r_{n}\rightarrow 0
arbitrarily slowly as
n\rightarrow\infty
) reveals that

\displaystyle\gamma_{n}-\gamma_{0}=-V_{n}^{-1}(\gamma_{n},\gamma_{n}^{\dagger},p_{W|Z})\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M(z_{i},\gamma_{n})^{\prime}\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma_{n})m(z_{i},\gamma_{0}),

which implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||n^{\frac{1}{4}}(\gamma_{n}-\gamma_{0})||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|V_{n}^{-1}(\gamma_{n},\gamma_{n}^{\dagger},p_{W|Z})\right|\right|_{op}

\displaystyle\quad\times\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}n^{\frac{1}{4}}\left|\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M(z_{i},\gamma_{n})^{\prime}\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma_{n})m(z_{i},\gamma_{0})\right|\right|_{2}

using
||Ax||_{2}\leq||A||_{op}\cdot||x||_{2}
for conformable matrix
A
and vector
x
. It thus suffices show that

(21)

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\left|V_{n}^{-1}(\gamma_{n},\gamma_{n}^{\dagger},p_{W|Z})\right|\right|_{op}=O(1)

and

(22)

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}n^{\frac{1}{4}}\left|\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M(z_{i},\gamma_{n})^{\prime}\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma_{n})m(z_{i},\gamma_{0})\right|\right|_{2}=o(1).

Indeed, these results complete the proof because they imply that

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||n^{\frac{1}{4}}(\gamma_{n}-\gamma_{0})||_{2}=o(1)

and, because
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
was fixed arbitrarily, it holds for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
.

Step 2.

I show (
21
). Since
\gamma_{n},\gamma_{n}^{\dagger}\in B(\gamma_{0},r_{n})
for
n
large, where
r_{n}\rightarrow 0
arbitrarily slowly as
n\rightarrow\infty
, a direct application of Lemma 6 establishes (
21
).

Step 3.

Applying the triangle inequality and
||Ax||_{2}\leq||A||_{op}||x||_{2}
for conformable matrix
A
and vector
x
,

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}n^{\frac{1}{4}}\left|\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M(z_{i},\gamma_{n})^{\prime}\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma_{n})m(z_{i},\gamma_{0})\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}n^{\frac{1}{4}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||M(z_{i},\gamma_{n})||_{op}\cdot||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma_{n})||_{op}\cdot||m(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{2}

for all
n\geq 1
. Next, an application of Hölder’s inequality and the submultiplicativity of the supremum yields that

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}n^{\frac{1}{4}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||M(z_{i},\gamma_{n})||_{op}\cdot||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma_{n})||_{op}\cdot||m(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma_{n})||_{op}\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||M(z_{i},\gamma_{n})||_{op}\cdot||m(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma_{n})||_{op}\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||M(z_{i},\gamma_{n})||_{op}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}n^{\frac{1}{4}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||m(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{2}^{2}}.

for all
n\geq 1
. Lemma 1 states that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\gamma,p_{W|Z})\in\Gamma\times\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}=O(1).

Next, since
\{\gamma_{n}(p_{W|Z}):p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}\subseteq\Gamma_{0}
for large
n
by Lemma 4, Assumption
10
.3(b) and the triangle inequality implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||M(z_{i},\gamma_{n})||_{op}^{2}}\leq o(1)+\sup_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}^{2}}.

Moreover, Assumption
6
.2 implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma)||_{op}^{2}}=O(1),

and, as a result, I can conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||M(z_{i},\gamma_{n})||_{op}^{2}}=O(1)

Finally, Assumption
5
and Assumption
10
.3(a) yields that

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}n^{\frac{1}{4}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}||m(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{2}^{2}}=o(1).

Putting these results together, I conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}n^{\frac{1}{4}}\left|\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M(z_{i},\gamma_{n})^{\prime}\Sigma^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma_{n})m(z_{i},\gamma_{0})\right|\right|_{2}=o(1).

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 8
.

Let
S_{n,i}(p_{W|Z})=\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W_{i},z_{i})-E_{P_{W|Z}}[\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)|Z=z_{i}]
for
i=1,...,n
. If Assumptions
4
,
5
,
6
,
7
,
8
,
9
, and
10
hold, then, for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\log L_{n}(p_{W|Z})-\log L_{n}(p_{t,n,W|Z})-t^{\prime}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}S_{n,i}(p_{W|Z})-\frac{1}{2}t^{\prime}V_{0}^{-1}t\right|\overset{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}{\longrightarrow}0,

where
\{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}_{n\geq 1}
are the sets defined in Assumption
10
and
p_{t,n,W|Z}\propto p_{W|Z}\exp(-t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0}/\sqrt{n})
.

Proof.

The proof has two steps. Step 1 establishes a uniform convergence result. Step 2 modifies the argument from Theorem 4.1 of
Castillo and Rousseau (2015)
to obtain the expansion.

Step 1.

I start by showing that

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||E_{P_{W|Z}}[\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)^{\prime}|Z=z_{i}]-E_{P_{0,W|Z}}[\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)^{\prime}|Z=z_{i}]||_{F}=o(1).

Using the definition of
\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)
and the submultiplicativity of the Frobenius norm, I know that

\displaystyle\left|\left|E_{P_{W|Z}}[\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)^{\prime}|Z=z_{i}]-E_{P_{0,W|Z}}[\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)^{\prime}|Z=z_{i}]\right|\right|_{F}

\displaystyle\quad=\left|\left|V_{0}^{-1}M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})^{\prime}\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})\left(\Sigma(z_{i},\gamma_{0})-\Sigma_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})\right)\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})V_{0}^{-1}\right|\right|_{F}

\displaystyle\quad\leq||V_{0}^{-1}||_{F}^{2}||M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{F}^{2}||\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{F}^{2}||\Sigma(z_{i},\gamma_{0})-\Sigma_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{F}

for
i=1,...,n
. Since
V_{0}
is real, symmetric, and positive definite by Assumptions
7
.1 and
9
.2, I know that there exists a constant
\underline{c}>0
such that

\displaystyle||V_{0}^{-1}||_{F}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{d_{\gamma}}\frac{1}{\lambda_{j}^{2}(V_{0})}\leq\frac{d_{\gamma}}{\lambda_{min}^{2}(V_{0})}\leq\frac{d_{\gamma}}{\underline{c}^{2}}

Moreover,
\sup_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}||M_{0}(z,\gamma_{0})||_{F}^{2}<\infty
and
\sup_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}||\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z,\gamma_{0})||^{2}_{F}<\infty
by Assumption
6
.2 and Assumption
7
. Consequently, there is a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||E_{P_{W|Z}}[\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)^{\prime}|Z=z_{i}]-E_{P_{0,W|Z}}[\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)^{\prime}|Z=z_{i}]||_{F}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||\Sigma(z_{i},\gamma_{0})-\Sigma_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})||_{F}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\sqrt{d_{g}}\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}||\Sigma(\cdot,\gamma_{0})-\Sigma_{0}(\cdot,\gamma_{0})||_{n,\infty},

where the last inequality uses that
||\Sigma(z,\gamma)||_{F}\leq\sqrt{d_{g}}||\Sigma(z,\gamma)||_{op}
. Assumption
10
.3(c) then implies

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||E_{P_{W|Z}}[\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)^{\prime}|Z=z_{i}]-E_{P_{0,W|Z}}[\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)^{\prime}|Z=z_{i}]||_{F}=o(1).

for all
p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
.

Step 2.

Using the definition of
p_{t,n,W|Z}
and Markov’s inequality, a sufficient condition for

\displaystyle P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}\left(\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\left|\log\left(\frac{L_{n}(p_{W|Z})}{L_{n}(p_{t,n,W|Z})}\right)-t^{\prime}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}S_{n,i}(p_{W|Z})-\frac{1}{2}t^{\prime}V_{0}^{-1}t\right|>\xi\right)=o(1)

for any
\xi>0
is

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log E_{P_{W|Z}}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\middle|Z=z_{i}\right]+t^{\prime}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{P_{W|Z}}\left[\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)\middle|Z=z_{i}\right]-\frac{1}{2}t^{\prime}V_{0}^{-1}t\right|=o(1)

uniformly over
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
. Using the series representation of the exponential function,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log E_{P_{W|Z}}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\middle|Z=z_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log\Bigg(1-t^{\prime}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}E_{P_{W|Z}}[\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)\mid Z=z_{i}]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\frac{1}{2}t^{\prime}\frac{1}{n}E_{P_{W|Z}}[\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)^{\prime}\mid Z=z_{i}]t+r_{n,i}(t,p_{W|Z})\Bigg)

for all
n\geq 1
, where

\displaystyle r_{n,i}(t,p_{W|Z})=E_{P_{W|Z}}\left[\sum_{l=3}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{l}(t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z))^{l}}{l!n^{\frac{l}{2}}}\middle|Z=z_{i}\right]

for all
i=1,...,n
. Applying the triangle inequality, I obtain

\displaystyle\left|r_{n,i}(t,p_{W|Z})\right|\leq\frac{1}{n^{\frac{3}{2}}}E_{P_{W|Z}}\left[\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\frac{|t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)|^{l}}{l!}\middle|Z=z_{i}\right]=\frac{1}{n^{\frac{3}{2}}}E_{P_{W|Z}}\left[\exp(|t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)|)\middle|Z=z_{i}\right]

for
i=1,...,n
with the equality using the series representation of the exponential. Consequently,

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\left|r_{n,i}(t,p_{W|Z})\right|\leq\frac{1}{n^{\frac{3}{2}}}\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}E_{P_{W|Z}}\left[\exp(|t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)|)\middle|Z=z_{i}\right].

As such, Assumption
10
.3(f) implies that
\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\left|r_{n,i}(t,p_{W|Z})\right|=O(n^{-\frac{3}{2}})
uniformly over
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
. Combining this result with that from Step 1, it follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log E_{P_{W|Z}}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\middle|Z=z_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log\left(1-t^{\prime}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}E_{P_{W|Z}}\left[\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)\middle|Z=z_{i}\right]+\frac{1}{2}t^{\prime}\frac{1}{n}E_{P_{0,W|Z}}\left[\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)^{\prime}\middle|Z=z_{i}\right]t\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\left.+O\left(\frac{1}{n^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)+o\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)\right)

uniformly over
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. A Taylor expansion of
x\mapsto\log(1+x)
around
x=0
then implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log E_{P_{W|Z}}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\middle|Z=z_{i}\right]

\displaystyle\quad=-t^{\prime}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{P_{W|Z}}\left[\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)\middle|Z=z_{i}\right]+\frac{1}{2}t^{\prime}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{P_{0,W|Z}}\left[\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)^{\prime}\middle|Z=z_{i}\right]t+o(1)

\displaystyle\quad=-t^{\prime}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{P_{W|Z}}\left[\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)\middle|Z=z_{i}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{1}{2}t^{\prime}V_{0}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})^{\prime}\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})\right)V_{0}^{-1}t+o(1)

\displaystyle\quad=-t^{\prime}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{P_{W|Z}}\left[\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)\middle|Z=z_{i}\right]+\frac{1}{2}t^{\prime}V_{0}^{-1}t+o(1)

uniformly over
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
. The second equality uses the definition of
\tilde{\chi}_{0}
and the linearity of expectation while the third equality uses the strong law of law numbers to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})^{\prime}\Sigma_{0}^{-1}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})M_{0}(z_{i},\gamma_{0})=V_{0}+o(1)

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Putting this all together, I conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log E_{P_{W|Z}}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\middle|Z=z_{i}\right]

\displaystyle\quad=-t^{\prime}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{P_{W|Z}}\left[\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)\middle|Z=z_{i}\right]+\frac{1}{2}t^{\prime}V_{0}^{-1}t+o(1)

uniformly over
\{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}_{n\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log E_{P_{W|Z}}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{t^{\prime}\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\middle|Z=z_{i}\right]+t^{\prime}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{P_{W|Z}}\left[\tilde{\chi}_{0}(W,Z)\middle|Z=z_{i}\right]-\frac{1}{2}t^{\prime}V_{0}^{-1}t\right|=o(1)

uniformly over
\{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\}_{n\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. This completes the proof.
∎

4.2.
Proofs of Lemmas Related to Propositions 1–3 and Matérn Example

Proof of Lemma
9
.

Let
C>0
be an arbitrary constant and let
R_{n}(\theta,B)=\log(L_{n}(p_{\theta,B})/L_{n}(p_{0,W|Z}))
. Applying Jensen’s inequality, I know that

\displaystyle\log\int\frac{L_{n}(p_{\theta,B})}{L_{n}(p_{0,W|Z})}d\bar{\Pi}_{n}(\theta,B)

\displaystyle\geq\int R_{n}(\theta,B)d\bar{\Pi}_{n}(\theta,B)

\displaystyle=\int\left\{R_{n}(\theta,B)-E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[R_{n}(\theta,B)\right]\right\}d\bar{\Pi}_{n}(\theta,B)

\displaystyle\quad+\int E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[R_{n}(\theta,B)\right]d\bar{\Pi}_{n}(\theta,B)

Since

\displaystyle E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[R_{n}(\theta,B)\right]=-\sum_{i=1}^{n}KL(p_{0,W|Z}(\cdot|z_{i}),p_{\theta,B}(\cdot|z_{i}))\geq-n\delta^{2}

for
\bar{\Pi}_{n}
-almost every
(\theta,B)
, it follows that

\displaystyle\int E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[R_{n}(\theta,B)\right]d\bar{\Pi}_{n}(\theta,B)\geq-n\delta^{2}.

As such, I know that

\displaystyle P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}\left(\int\frac{L_{n}(p_{\theta,B})}{L_{n}(p_{0,W|Z})}d\bar{\Pi}_{n}(\theta,B)\leq\exp\left(-(1+C)n\delta^{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}\left(\int\left\{R_{n}(\theta,B)-E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[R_{n}(\theta,B)\right]\right\}d\bar{\Pi}_{n}(\theta,B)\leq-Cn\delta^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}\left(\left|\int\left\{R_{n}(\theta,B)-E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[R_{n}(\theta,B)\right]\right\}d\bar{\Pi}_{n}(\theta,B)\right|\geq Cn\delta^{2}\right)

Applying Chebyshev’s inequality, I know that

\displaystyle P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}\left(\left|\int\left\{R_{n}(\theta,B)-E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[R_{n}(\theta,B)\right]\right\}d\bar{\Pi}_{n}(\theta,B)\right|\geq Cn\delta^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{C^{2}n^{2}\delta^{4}}E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[\left|\int\left\{R_{n}(\theta,B)-E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[R_{n}(\theta,B)\right]\right\}d\bar{\Pi}_{n}(\theta,B)\right|^{2}\right]

Applying Jensen’s inequality and then Fubini’s theorem,

\displaystyle E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[\left|\int\left\{R_{n}(\theta,B)-E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[R_{n}(\theta,B)\right]\right\}d\bar{\Pi}_{n}(\theta,B)\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int Var_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[R_{n}(\theta,B)\right]d\bar{\Pi}_{n}(\theta,B)

\displaystyle\quad\leq n\delta^{2},

where the last inequality uses that

\displaystyle Var_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[R_{n}(\theta,B)\right]=\sum_{i=1}^{n}KLV\left(p_{0,W|Z}(\cdot|z_{i}),p_{\theta,B}(\cdot|z_{i})\right)\leq n\delta^{2}

for
\bar{\Pi}_{n}
-almost every
(\theta,B)
. As such,

\displaystyle P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}\left(\left|\int\left\{R_{n}(\theta,B)-E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[R_{n}(\theta,B)\right]\right\}d\bar{\Pi}_{n}(\theta,B)\right|\geq Cn\delta^{2}\right)\leq\frac{1}{C^{2}n\delta^{2}}.

Since this argument holds for
C>0
fixed arbitrarily, it extends to any
C>0
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
10
.

Let
\bar{\Pi}_{n}
be the renormalized restriction of
\Pi
to
\mathcal{U}_{n}(p_{0,W|Z},\delta_{n})
and let
A_{n}
be the event

\displaystyle\int\frac{L_{n}(p_{\theta,B})}{L_{n}(p_{0,W|Z})}d\bar{\Pi}_{n}(\theta,B)\geq\exp\left(-(1+C)n\delta^{2}_{n}\right).

for some small constant
C>0
. By Lemma
9
,
P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}(A_{n})\rightarrow 1
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, so it suffices to show

\displaystyle E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[\Pi(\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}^{c}|W^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\{A_{n}\}\right]\longrightarrow 0

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Using the definition of the posterior and
A_{n}
and that
\Pi(\mathcal{U}_{n}(p_{0,W|Z},\delta_{n}))\geq\breve{C}\exp(-n\delta_{n}^{2})
, it follows that

\displaystyle E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[\Pi(\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}^{c}|W^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\{A_{n}\}\right]\leq\breve{C}\exp((2+C)n\delta_{n}^{2})E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[\int_{\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}^{c}}\frac{L_{n}(p_{\theta,B})}{L_{n}(p_{0,W|Z})}d\Pi(\theta,B)\right]

Applying Fubini’s theorem, I know that

\displaystyle E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[\int_{\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}^{c}}\frac{L_{n}(p_{\theta,B})}{L_{n}(p_{0,W|Z})}d\Pi(\theta,B)\right]=\int_{\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}^{c}}E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left(\frac{L_{n}(p_{\theta,B})}{L_{n}(p_{0,W|Z})}\right)d\Pi(\theta,B)=\Pi(\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}^{c}).

Consequently, the assumption
\Pi(\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}^{c})\leq\check{C}\exp(-\bar{C}n\delta_{n}^{2})
implies that

\displaystyle E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[\Pi(\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}^{c}|W^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\{A_{n}\}\right]\leq\breve{C}\check{C}\exp(((2+C)-\bar{C})n\delta_{n}^{2})\rightarrow 0

as
n\rightarrow\infty
because
n\delta_{n}^{2}\rightarrow\infty
as
n\rightarrow\infty
and
\bar{C}>2+C
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
11
.

The proof has two steps. Step 1 shows it is enough to study
\Pi(\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\cap\{h_{n,2}(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z})\geq D\delta_{n}\}|W^{(n)})
on an event
A_{n}
satisfying
P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}(A_{n})\rightarrow 1
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Step 2 uses this and the entropy restriction (
3
) to conclude the lemma.

Step 1.

Conditions (
1
) and (
2
) imply that it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\Pi(\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\cap\{h_{n,2}(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z})\geq D\delta_{n}\}|W^{(n)})\overset{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}{\longrightarrow}0

as
n\rightarrow\infty
because Lemma
10
implies that if these two conditions hold then

\displaystyle\Pi(h_{n,2}(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z})\geq D\delta_{n}|W^{(n)})=\Pi(\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\cap\{h_{n,2}(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z})\geq D\delta_{n}\}|W^{(n)})+o_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}(1).

To this end, let
\bar{\Pi}_{n}
be the renormalized restriction of
\Pi
to
\mathcal{U}_{n}(p_{0,W|Z},\delta_{n})
and let
A_{n}
be the event

\displaystyle\int\frac{L_{n}(p_{\theta,B})}{L_{n}(p_{0,W|Z})}d\bar{\Pi}_{n}(\theta,B)\geq\exp\left(-(1+C)n\delta^{2}_{n}\right).

for some small constant
C>0
. Lemma
9
implies that
P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}(A_{n}^{c})\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, and, as a result,

\displaystyle E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[\Pi(\{h_{n,2}(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z})\geq D\delta_{n}\}\cap\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}|W^{(n)})\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[\Pi(\{h_{n,2}(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z})\geq D\delta_{n}\}\cap\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}|W^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\{A_{n}\}\right]+o(1),

meaning that it suffices to show that

(23)

\displaystyle E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[\Pi(\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\cap\{h_{n,2}(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z})\geq D\delta_{n}\}|W^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\{A_{n}\}\right]\longrightarrow 0

as
n\rightarrow\infty
to complete the proof.

Step 2.

I show (
23
). Let
\{\phi_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
be a sequence of tests with
0\leq\phi_{n}(W^{(n)})\leq 1
such that, for any
\delta>0
and any
p_{\theta,B}
with
h_{n,2}(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z})>\delta
, the following holds

(24)

\displaystyle E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}[\phi_{n}(W^{(n)})]\leq\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}n\delta^{2}\right)

and

(25)

\displaystyle\sup_{(\tilde{\theta},\tilde{B}):h_{n,2}(p_{\tilde{\theta},\tilde{B}},p_{\theta,B})\leq\delta/18}E_{P_{\theta,B}^{(n)}}[1-\phi_{n}(W^{(n)})]\leq\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}n\delta^{2}\right),

where
P_{\theta,B}^{(n)}
being the probability measure associated with the product density
\prod_{i=1}^{n}p_{\theta,B}(\cdot|z_{i})
. Lemma 2 of
Ghosal and van der Vaart (2007)
establishes the existence of such tests. If

(26)

\displaystyle\sup_{\delta>\delta_{n}}\log N\left(\frac{\delta}{36},\{p_{\theta,B}\in\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}:h_{n,2}(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z})<\delta\},h_{n}\right)\leq\tilde{C}n\delta_{n}^{2}

for some constant
\tilde{C}>0
, then Lemma 9 of
Ghosal and van der Vaart (2007)
states that (
24
) and (
25
) implies

(27)

\displaystyle E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}[\phi_{n}(W^{(n)})]\leq\exp(\tilde{C}n\delta_{n}^{2})\frac{\exp(-\frac{1}{2}D^{2}n\delta_{n}^{2})}{1-\exp(-\frac{1}{2}D^{2}n\delta_{n}^{2})}

and

(28)

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{\theta,B}\in\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\cap\{(\theta,B):h_{n,2}(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z})\geq D\delta_{n}\}}E_{P_{\theta,B}^{(n)}}[1-\phi_{n}(W^{(n)})]\leq\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}D^{2}n\delta_{n}^{2}\right)

Since
\{p_{\theta,B}\in\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}:h_{n,2}(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z})<D\delta_{n}\}\subseteq\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}
, the approximate monotonicity of covering numbers under set inclusion (see 4.2.10 in
Vershynin (2018)
) leads to the conclusion that
\log N(\delta_{n}/72,\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z},h_{n})\leq\tilde{C}n\delta_{n}^{2}
implies (
26
). Consequently, condition (
3
) guarantees (
27
) and (
28
). Recognizing that
1=1-\phi_{n}(W^{(n)})+\phi_{n}(W^{(n)})
and that probabilities are bounded by one, I can then conclude that

\displaystyle E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[\Pi(\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\cap\{h_{n,2}(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z})\geq D\delta_{n}\}|W^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\{A_{n}\}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}[\phi_{n}(W^{(n)})]

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\exp((2+C)n\delta_{n}^{2})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[\int_{\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\cap\{(\theta,B):h_{n,2}(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z})\geq D\delta_{n}\}}(1-\phi_{n}(W^{(n)}))\frac{L_{n}(p_{\theta,B})}{L_{n}(p_{0,W|Z})}d\Pi(\theta,B)\right].

Applying Fubini’s theorem, it follows that

\displaystyle E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[\int_{\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\cap\{(\theta,B):h_{n,2}(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z})\geq D\delta_{n}\}}(1-\phi_{n}(W^{(n)}))\frac{L_{n}(p_{\theta,B})}{L_{n}(p_{0,W|Z})}d\Pi(\theta,B)\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{p_{W|Z}\in\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\cap\{(\theta,B):h_{n,2}(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z})\geq D\delta_{n}\}}E_{P_{\theta,B}^{(n)}}\left[1-\phi_{n}(W^{(n)})\right]

So by setting
D>0
large, it follows from (
27
), (
28
), and
n\delta_{n}^{2}\rightarrow\infty
that

\displaystyle E_{P_{0,W|Z}^{(n)}}\left[\Pi(\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{n,W|Z}\cap\{h_{n,2}(p_{\theta,B},p_{0,W|Z})\geq D\delta_{n}\}|W^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\{A_{n}\}\right]\longrightarrow 0

as
n\rightarrow\infty
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
12
.

The proof has two steps. The first establishes that an initial concentration result using a matrix Bernstein-type inequality. Step 2 uses this result to establish the inequalities.

Step 1.

Let
X_{i}=\psi^{J_{n}}(Z_{i})\psi^{J_{n}}(Z_{i})^{\prime}-E_{P_{0,Z}}[\psi^{J_{n}}(Z)\psi^{J_{n}}(Z)^{\prime}]
for
i=1,...,n
. Observing that
\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
forms an i.i.d sequence,
n^{-1}\Psi_{J_{n}}^{\prime}\Psi_{J_{n}}-E_{P_{0,Z}}[\psi^{J_{n}}(Z)\psi^{J_{n}}(Z)^{\prime}]=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}
,
X_{i}
is symmetric, and
E_{P_{0,Z}}[X_{1}]=0
, I can apply Theorem 4.1 of
Chen and Christensen (2015)
to conclude that for any
\xi>0
,

\displaystyle P_{0,Z}^{\infty}\left(\left|\left|\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J_{n}}^{\prime}\Psi_{J_{n}}-E_{P_{0,Z}}[\psi^{J_{n}}(Z)\psi^{J_{n}}(Z)^{\prime}]\right|\right|_{op}\geq\xi\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2d(J_{n})\exp\left(-\frac{n^{2}\xi^{2}/2}{s_{n}^{2}+R_{n}n\xi/3}\right),

where
s_{n}^{2}=n||E_{P_{0,Z}}[X^{2}_{1}]||_{op}
, and
\max_{1\leq i\leq n}||X_{i}||_{op}\leq R_{n}
. Using the properties of CDV wavelets and that
p_{0,Z}
is bounded away from zero and infinity (by Assumption
11
), one can show
d(J_{n})=O(2^{J_{n}d_{z}})
,
s_{n}^{2}=O(n2^{J_{n}d_{z}})
, and
R_{n}=O(2^{J_{n}d_{z}})
. Consequently, for every
\xi>0
, there are constants
\tilde{c}_{1}>0
and
\tilde{c}_{2}>0
(with
\tilde{c}_{2}
depending on
\xi
) such that

\displaystyle P_{0,Z}^{\infty}\left(\left|\left|\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J_{n}}^{\prime}\Psi_{J_{n}}-E_{P_{0,Z}}[\psi^{J_{n}}(Z)\psi^{J_{n}}(Z)^{\prime}]\right|\right|_{op}\geq\xi\right)\leq\tilde{c}_{1}2^{J_{n}d_{z}}\exp\left(-\frac{\tilde{c}_{2}n}{2^{J_{n}d_{z}}}\right)=:a_{n}

for
n
large. Since
\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}a_{n}<\infty
for
2^{J_{n}}=(n/\log n)^{1/(2\alpha+d_{z})}
, it follows that, for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
,

(29)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J_{n}}^{\prime}\Psi_{J_{n}}-E_{P_{0,Z}}[\psi^{J_{n}}(Z)\psi^{J_{n}}(Z)^{\prime}]\right|\right|_{op}\longrightarrow 0

as
n\rightarrow\infty
by the Borel-Cantelli lemma.

Step 2.

By (
29
), I know that, for
P_{0,Z}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{min}\left(\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J_{n}}^{\prime}\Psi_{J_{n}}\right)\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{min}\left(E_{P_{0,Z}}[\psi^{J_{n}}(Z)\psi^{J_{n}}(Z)^{\prime}]\right)

for
n
large. Using that
\{\psi_{jk}\}_{j,k}
is an orthonormal basis for
L^{2}([0,1]^{d_{z}})
, I know that for
v\neq 0
,

\displaystyle v^{\prime}E_{P_{0,Z}}[\psi^{J_{n}}(Z)\psi^{J_{n}}(Z)^{\prime}]v

\displaystyle\geq\int(v^{\prime}\psi^{J_{n}}(z))^{2}p_{0,Z}(z)dz

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}p_{0,Z}(z)\int(v^{\prime}\psi^{J_{n}}(z))^{2}dz

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}p_{0,Z}(z)||v||^{2}_{2}\lambda_{min}(I)

Consequently,

\displaystyle\lambda_{min}\left(\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J_{n}}^{\prime}\Psi_{J_{n}}\right)\geq\frac{1}{2}\inf_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}p_{0,Z}(z)

for
n
large. Set
c_{1}=\inf_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}p_{0,Z}(z)/2>0
to obtain the first claim (with positivity following the boundedness of
p_{0,Z}
away from zero and infinity). As such, it remains to establish the second inequality. Observe that

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{jk}(z_{i})=\psi_{jk}(z_{i})-\hat{\tau}_{n,jk}^{\prime}\psi_{-jk}^{J_{n}}(z_{i})=\hat{v}_{n,jk}^{\prime}\psi^{J}(z_{i})

for each
i=1,...,n
, where
\hat{v}_{n,jk}
is a vector that comprises an appropriate ordering of the elements of
(1,-\hat{\tau}_{n,jk}^{\prime})^{\prime}
. Squaring and summing over
i
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\psi}_{jk}^{2}(z_{i})=\hat{v}_{n,jk}^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J_{n}}^{\prime}\Psi_{J_{n}}\right)\hat{v}_{n,jk}\geq||\hat{v}_{n,jk}||_{2}^{2}\lambda_{min}\left(\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{J_{n}}^{\prime}\Psi_{J_{n}}\right)\geq c_{1}||\hat{v}_{n,jk}||_{2}^{2}

for
n
large. Using
||\hat{v}_{n,jk}||_{2}^{2}=||\hat{\tau}_{n,jk}||_{2}^{2}+1\geq 1
for all
j,k
, I obtain

\displaystyle\min_{0\leq j\leq J_{n}}\min_{0\leq k\leq 2^{jd_{z}}-1}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\psi}_{jk}(z_{i})^{2}\geq c_{1}

for
n
large. Set
c_{2}=c_{1}
to conclude the proof.
∎

Lemma 13
.

Suppose
\{w_{\theta}:\theta\in\Theta\}\subseteq C^{\beta}([0,1]^{d},\mathbb{R})\cap S^{\beta}([0,1]^{d},\mathbb{R})
for some
\beta>0
,
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}||w_{\theta}||_{\beta}<\infty
, and
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}||w_{\theta}||_{2,2,\beta}<\infty
. Then, for
\delta\in(0,1)
, the following is true:

1.

If
\beta\leq\alpha
, then

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}:||f-w_{\theta}||_{\infty}<\delta}\frac{1}{2}||f||_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\lesssim\delta^{-(2\alpha+d-2\beta)/\beta}

2.

If
\alpha<\beta<\alpha+d/2
, then

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}:||f-w_{\theta}||_{\infty}<\delta}\frac{1}{2}||f||_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\lesssim\delta^{-\frac{d}{\beta}}

3.

If
\beta\geq\alpha+d/2
, then
\{w_{\theta}:\theta\in\Theta\}\subseteq\mathcal{H}
and

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}:||f-w_{\theta}||_{\infty}<\delta}\frac{1}{2}||f||_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}<\infty

where
(\mathcal{H},\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\mathcal{H}})
is the RKHS of the Matérn process.

Proof.

The proof follows a similar argument of Lemma 4 in
van der Vaart and van Zanten (2011)
with appropriate modifications that account for uniformity over
\Theta
.

Step 1.

I start by constructing a higher order kernel
K
. It is the same as that from Lemma 4 of
van der Vaart and van Zanten (2011)
, and, for the sake of clarity, I outline its construction. Let
k:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a function with a real, symmetric Fourier transform
\hat{k}
, which equals
1/2\pi
in a neighborhood of zero and has compact support. Using
\hat{k}(\lambda)=(2\pi)^{-1}\int\exp(i\lambda u)k(u)du
, one can show
\int k(t)dt=1
and
\int(it)^{l}k(t)dt=0
for all integers
l\geq 1
, where, in this lemma
i
, is the imaginary unit. Defining
K(t)=k(t_{1})\cdots k(t_{d})
for
t=(t_{1},...,t_{d})^{\prime}
, it follows that
K
integrates to one and has finite and vanishing moments of all orders greater than zero, thereby defining a higher order kernel.

Step 2.

For
h>0
, let
K_{h}(t)=h^{-d}K(t/h)
for each
t=(t_{1},...,t_{d})^{\prime}
(with
t/h=(t_{1}/h,...,t_{d}/h)^{\prime}
), and
f_{\theta,h}=K_{h}*w_{\theta}
(i.e.,
f_{\theta,h}(t)=\int K_{h}(t-\tilde{t})w_{\theta}(\tilde{t})d\tilde{t}
). I show that
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}||w_{\theta}-f_{\theta,h}||_{\infty}\leq\tilde{C}(\beta,K,\Theta)h^{\beta}
, where
\tilde{C}(\beta,K,\Theta)
is a constant that depends on
\beta
,
K
, and
\Theta
. Since
K_{h}
is a higher order kernel and
w_{\theta}\in C^{\beta}([0,1]^{d},\mathbb{R})
, I can apply Proposition 4.3.33(ii) of
Giné and Nickl (2016)
to conclude
||w_{\theta}-f_{\theta,h}||_{\infty}\leq C(\beta,K)||D^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}w_{\theta}||_{\infty}h^{\beta}
, where
C(\beta,K)
is a constant that depends on
\beta
and
K
only. Since
||D^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}w_{\theta}||_{\infty}\leq||w_{\theta}||_{\beta}
by the definition of
||\cdot||_{\beta}
and
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}||w_{\theta}||_{\beta}<\infty
by assumption, set
\tilde{C}(\beta,K,\Theta)=C(\beta,K)\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}||w_{\theta}||_{\beta}
.

Step 3.

I now use the result from Step 2 to conclude the lemma with three substeps that conform to the parts of the lemma.

Step 3A.

I start with
\beta\leq\alpha
. I can write
f_{\theta,h}(t)=\int\exp(i\lambda^{\prime}t)\hat{f}_{\theta,h}(\lambda)(1+||\lambda||_{2}^{2})^{\alpha+d/2}d\mu(\lambda)
, where
\hat{f}_{\theta,h}(\lambda)=\hat{K}(h\lambda)\hat{w}_{\theta}(\lambda)
is the Fourier transform of
f_{\theta,h}
,
\hat{K}
is the Fourier transform of
K
,
\hat{w}_{\theta}
is the Fourier transform of
w_{\theta}
, and
d\mu(\lambda)=(1+||\lambda||_{2}^{2})^{-\alpha-d/2}d\lambda
. Consequently, using the definition of
||\cdot||_{\mathcal{H}}
for the Matérn process (cf. (12) in
van der Vaart and van Zanten (2011)
), I obtain

\displaystyle||f_{\theta,h}||_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\leq\int|\hat{K}(h\lambda)\hat{w}_{\theta}(\lambda)|^{2}(1+||\lambda||_{2}^{2})^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}}d\lambda

By dividing and multiplying by
(1+||\lambda||_{2}^{2})^{\beta}
inside the integral, applying Hölder’s inequality, and using the definition of
||\cdot||_{2,2,\beta}
, I obtain that

\displaystyle\int|\hat{K}(h\lambda)\hat{w}_{\theta}(\lambda)|^{2}(1+||\lambda||_{2}^{2})^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}}d\lambda

\displaystyle=\int|\hat{K}(h\lambda)|^{2}(1+||\lambda||_{2}^{2})^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}-\beta}\cdot|\hat{w}_{\theta}(\lambda)|^{2}(1+||\lambda||_{2}^{2})^{\beta}d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\lambda}\left(|\hat{K}(h\lambda)|^{2}(1+||\lambda||_{2}^{2})^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}-\beta}\right)||w_{\theta}||_{2,2,\beta}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\lambda}\left(|\hat{K}(h\lambda)|^{2}(1+||\lambda||_{2}^{2})^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}-\beta}\right)\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}||w_{\theta}||_{2,2,\beta}^{2}

for all
\theta\in\Theta
. Dividing and multiplying by
(1+||h\lambda||_{2}^{2})^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}-\beta}
within the supremum and writing
\tilde{\lambda}=h\lambda
, it follows that

\displaystyle||f_{\theta,h}||_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\leq\bar{C}(\alpha,\beta,d,h)\tilde{C}(\alpha,\beta,d,\Theta),

where

\displaystyle\bar{C}(\alpha,\beta,d,h)=\sup_{\lambda}\left(\frac{1+||\lambda||_{2}^{2}}{1+||h\lambda||_{2}^{2}}\right)^{\alpha+\frac{d}{2}-\beta}\leq\left(\frac{1}{h}\right)^{\alpha+d/2-\beta}

for
0<h\leq 1
, and
